| D91 | **More page components, each added through one registry: separator lines, dual buttons, accordions, tabs, FAQs, videos, HTML, testimonials, social media buttons and icon lists, and carousels.** A newer kind of component is a block type (`PageBlock`, its schema, `newBlock()`, `blockHasContent()`, `blockText()`, `PageBlockView`) plus an entry in `BLOCK_EDITORS` (`src/components/admin/block-fields.tsx`: its title, General and Style fields); the builder's generic dialog adds the font, spacing, frame and Advanced tab they share, and its texts are listed with labels in `mapBlockTexts()`, from which translating builds its fields. Components made of items (tabs, questions, testimonials) edit them with `ItemsEditor`; each item keeps an id, by which its texts' translations are kept. A separator line is an `hr` of its own style (solid, dashed, dotted, double), thickness, colour (the site's border colour unless chosen), width (10–100 % of its column) and place. A dual button is two links side by side (`dualButton`), each with its own text, address, new tab, style and colours, sharing size, corners, weight, the space between and their place; one under another on phones if set, and each shown once it has text and an address (`buttonShows()`). An accordion's sections (`PanelItem`: a title and rich text) are the browser's own `details`, so they open without script and the browser's find opens the one holding a word; the first can start open, one-at-a-time is a shared `name`, and it looks like lines between or boxes. Its words are the page's (`blockText()`: excerpt, llms.txt, the chat agent's knowledge). Tabs share the accordion's items and follow the ARIA tabs pattern (`TabsView`: arrow keys, Home and End choose as they move focus); every panel is in the page, the others hidden, so search engines read them all; they look underlined, as pills or as boxes, placed at the start, centred or spread across. FAQs (`faq`) are questions and answers drawn as the accordion is, a question shown once it has an answer (`faqShows()`), with schema.org's FAQPage (`faqJsonLd()`) beside them unless switched off, for search engines and AI assistants. A video (`video`) is one uploaded to the site (`VideoUploadButton`, played by the browser with or without its controls; one that starts by itself starts muted, loops, and stays still for visitors who prefer less motion) or one on YouTube or Vimeo, given by its share address (`embedUrl()`, `src/lib/video-embed.ts`). An outside video is a facade until pressed: its poster (a picture from the site, never fetched from YouTube or Vimeo) or a plain panel, a play button saying where it plays from, and only then the player, from youtube-nocookie.com or Vimeo with `dnt=1`, so nothing reaches either before the visitor chooses to play and no consent is needed. Its shape is a ratio (16:9 unless chosen) and its title names the player for screen readers and is translated. HTML (`html`) is the owner's own code, styles and scripts (up to 50,000 characters), run in a sandboxed frame without `allow-same-origin` (`HtmlFrame`, `src/lib/html-frame.ts`): its own origin, so nothing in it reaches the site's or the admin's cookies, storage or session, which is why it may run on stores before they have their own hosts where the owner's page code (D61) may not. A script in the frame reports its content's height, which the frame takes (up to 10,000 px) unless a height is set, and the page sends it its font and text colour. Content from another service can wait for the visitor to press Show, so nothing loads before they ask. Its code is the same in every language; only its title, naming the frame, is translated, and none of its words are the page's. On the builder's canvas frames take no pointer, so the block is chosen and dragged as any other. Testimonials (`testimonials`) are what customers said, written in by the owner (words, name, title or place, 1–5 stars, a picture), drawn by `TestimonialCards` as cards, plain or large quotes in one to four columns (one on phones): each a `figure` whose `blockquote` is what was said and whose caption is who said it, the stars read out as words (`StarRating`). What was said and who they are are translated, a name never; the words are the page's. Set to Google (`source: "google"`), they are the business's rating and reviews on Google (`GoogleReviewsSection`, `src/server/google-reviews.ts`): the owner's Google Maps Platform key, encrypted like payment secrets, and their business, found and chosen by name, are kept per owner in `commerce.google_places` (a store's under Integrations, owners only; Kaizen's under Platform), and the reviews are asked of the Places API (New) as the page is shown, in its language, inside `<Suspense>` and never kept, as Google's terms allow no caching; each page view showing them is one request on the owner's Google account. Only the business's id and the language are sent. Google is named as their source with a link to the business on Google Maps, each reviewer's name links to their profile there, and reviewers' pictures are left out, as loading them from Google would tell it about the visitor. A component can show only reviews with enough stars, and fewer than Google's five; Google's words are not the page's. A content grid or testimonials can show as a carousel (`display: "carousel"`, `Carousel`): the same list in one row that scrolls sideways with the browser's own scrolling, snapping to a tile (`[data-carousel-track]` in globals.css), as many tiles to a screen as the columns (a grid can show a quarter of the next, `peek`), with previous and next arrows moving a screenful and off at either end. Nothing moves by itself, touch and trackpads scroll it, and tabbing to a tile's link brings it into view, so no tile is out of reach without the arrows. Social media buttons (`socialLinks`, `SocialLinksView`, `src/lib/social-links.ts`) link to the business's profiles on thirty networks, plus email, phone and website: each its logo (Simple Icons, CC0, in `BrandIcon`, LinkedIn's drawn in since Simple Icons dropped it), named for screen readers or in words, opening in a new tab with `rel="me"`; as icons, filled or outlined circles, rounded or square, in each network's colour (black marks take the text's, so they show on dark themes; filled ones get black or white by `textOn()`), the theme's accent or one chosen. What the owner types becomes the address (`socialHref()`: https added, an email address as `mailto:`, a number as `tel:`), and only such addresses are kept. An icon list (`iconList`, `IconListView`) is lines of words, each after one of 66 icons (`ICONS` in `src/lib/icons.ts`, drawn by `ListIcon` from Lucide, ISC, imported by name so only those ship), one under another or side by side, in the theme's accent or a colour chosen, a line a link if given an address; the icons are hidden from screen readers, as the words say what each line means, and the words are the page's and translated line by line. | 2026-09-28 | Asked for by the owner, with tabs, accordions, dual buttons, HTML, FAQs, testimonials, video and a carousel. One registry keeps a component's editor in one place instead of eight across a 4,700-line builder, and translation from the texts' own list means no component can be forgotten there. |

It may come from another provider than the rest, with its own address and key (`image_provider`, `image_base_url`, `image_api_key_encrypted`, kept like the other key), and a quality where the model takes one. `generateImage()` asks the OpenAI-compatible `/images/generations`, which OpenAI and others offer, for a shape (landscape, portrait or square, as each model's nearest size); a size or quality a model refuses is left out and remembered, as for text. | D102 | **Subscription boxes (first built as weekly deliveries): signed-in shoppers keep a standing list, which becomes each delivery's order at the cutoff and is charged to their saved card when the delivery is sent.** A module stores switch on under Features (`deliveries` in `stores.modules`). The store sets delivery days (`delivery_schedules`): one weekday in one market, and a cutoff 1–7 days before at a time in the store's time zone. A shopper starts from Weekly delivery in My account (`/s/{store}/{market}/deliveries`): they choose a day, give the address and tick that their card is charged for each delivery when it is on its way, for what it holds at that day's prices, until they end it; Stripe Checkout in setup mode on the store's connected account then saves the card (a Stripe customer there, never `payment_method_types`), and the list (`standing_orders`, one open per customer) is on, confirmed by email. At each cutoff, the five-minute job turns every list agreed to before it into that delivery's order (`standing_deliveries`, once per list and day): its lines capped to the stock there is, placed through `placeOrder()` from a cart of its own, so prices, VAT, shipping and stock locks are the checkout's; its stock is held until the day after the delivery, and the shopper is emailed what it holds, what it costs and what was left out. A day skipped, a paused list or an empty one is recorded without an order. A change made after a cutoff first makes that delivery (as the list stood) and then goes into the next, so a delivery being packed never changes. The order waits for payment until staff mark it sent: Mark sent then charges its total to the saved card off session, with Kaizen's fee, and only a successful charge marks it paid and sent. A refused card (or a bank asking the shopper to confirm) leaves the order waiting and emails the shopper a link to pay by Stripe Checkout, whose card then becomes the list's; an open link is closed before staff try the card again, so nothing is paid twice. Shoppers skip coming days, pause, resume or end the list (ending lets go of the card; a delivery already being packed is still sent and charged), and staff cancel a delivery not yet sent without a charge. Deleting the customer account deletes its lists and cards; the orders stay. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner for a grocery box. Stripe Billing needs a fixed amount per period, so a list that changes every week is charged by Kaizen with Stripe's saved-card payments (as the no-show fee, D66), which also lets the charge be what was packed. Charging when it is sent follows the grocery practice of paying for what arrives. Prices are the day of the cutoff's, and the shopper is told the amount at the cutoff, before anything is charged. | D108 | **Customer groups with a fixed discount, and company accounts whose employees are invited by email.** A store makes *customer groups* (`commerce.customer_tiers`: a name and a whole percentage, 1–100; `/admin/{store}/customer-groups`), such as Wholesale at 10 %, and puts customers in one (`customers.tier_id`, from the group's page by email or from the customer's own page; someone with no account yet gets one made ahead of them, its email not proven, so they prove it when they sign in). A *company* (`commerce.customer_companies`; `/admin/{store}/companies`) has a group, a share of the group's discount its employees get (`employee_share_percent`, 100 is all of it, 50 half; the main account always gets all of it), a limit on accounts (`max_members`) and an optional organisation number; a customer belongs to at most one company (`customers.company_id`, `company_role` `owner` for the main account or `employee`). The discount is read from the membership on every purchase (`memberDiscountFor()` in `src/server/customer-tiers.ts`, the pure rule `memberDiscount()` in `src/lib/customer-tiers.ts`): the better of the customer's own group and their company's, none for a group or company switched off, so taking someone out of a company stops it at once. It comes off everything bought once (goods, downloads, appointments, stays, rentals), not subscriptions, sign-up fees or shipping, as the lowered unit price a subscription's discount is (`planPrice()`), before any discount code, which then counts the lowered prices; free shipping still counts the basket at list price. `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()` work it out the same way (`checkout-kinds.int.test.ts` holds them together with a group's member and a company's employee); the order keeps its part of the discount (`orders.member_discount_minor`, `member_label`, `member_percent`, and `order_lines.member_discount_minor`), and Stripe takes it in the same one-time coupon as a code (named after the company or group, and the code when both). A company's main account invites employees from My account, My company (`/s/{store}/{market}/account/company`: several addresses at once, each an email with a link; up to 20 at a time, 50 a day, the company's limit counting invitations waiting; sending to an address with an invitation open sends it again with a new link, and the old one stops working), sees its accounts and invitations, withdraws an invitation (`revokeInvite()`) or removes an employee (`removeMember()`) at any time; an employee can leave. The link opens `/s/{store}/{market}/account/company/invite/{token}`, a page with a button: nothing changes until it is pressed, so an email scanner accepts nothing. Accepting (`acceptInvite()`, one transaction under a lock) opens the account if there is none, or joins the existing one (an account whose email nobody had proven loses a password chosen before, as it may not have been theirs; a proven account keeps its password), proves the email (the link is what proves it), claims orders placed with it, and emails a confirmation with a link that signs in once (`customer_sign_in_links`, a hash kept, valid a week; its page `/account/sign-in/{token}` also uses a button). An account already in another company here cannot accept until it has left that one. A company or main account emails the person when they are removed. Invitations are kept a year after they end, links a month after they expire (daily cron). Tokens are only ever kept as SHA-256 hashes. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: a B2B store giving a group of customers a fixed 10 % discount by tier, and companies whose main account invites employees, who then get the same discount or a share of it, also if they already have an account, with the invitation revocable at any time as employees leave. | D109 | **Language and currency are chosen apart: a store offers any languages and any currencies, whichever countries it sells to, and a shopper picks both.** A *market* stays a country (shipping, VAT, prices), but the address now says how it is shown: `/s/{store}/no` is Norway in its own language and currency, `no-en` English, `no-eur` euro, `no-en-eur` both (`src/lib/market-slug.ts`: two letters are a language, three a currency, a country's own left out, so every old address still works and each view is one cacheable, indexable address). A store's languages are `stores.locales` (the main one first, one variant per language; never fewer than each country's own default language, which the owner sets per country in `markets.default_locale`), its currencies `commerce.store_currencies` (a rate in units per 1 EUR, as the ECB publishes them, a rounding step and an order); until an owner chooses, both are what its countries have, so nothing changed for existing stores (`localizationOf()` in `src/lib/localization.ts`). Prices, shipping, free-shipping limits, discount amounts, fees and every other stored amount stay in each country's own currency, and are converted when read (`Market.conversion`, `shown()` in `src/lib/markets.ts`; `convertedSql()` in `src/server/product-conditions.ts` for filters, sorting and search, which use the same factor and step as the code): `Math.round(amount × rate(to) / rate(from) × 10^(digits(to) − digits(from)) / step) × step`, in minor units, the euro being the pivot. A currency without a rate cannot be converted into or out of, and is not offered; converting to a country's own currency needs no rate. `Market` carries what is shown (`currency`, `locale`) and what is kept (`nativeCurrency`, `ownLocale`), so the ~300 places that read `market.currency` and `market.locale` show the shopper's choice without change, and code that writes an amount down (a subscription, a weekly delivery) reads `nativeCurrency`; subscriptions and weekly deliveries are only sold in a country's own currency (shown nowhere else, `needsNativeCurrency`, and the deliveries page redirects to the native view). The cart, checkout and order all record the currency shown, `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()` convert the same way and Stripe is sent that currency (`checkout-kinds.int.test.ts` takes goods, a stay, an appointment, a rental, a code and a company's discount through checkout in euro and holds cart, order and Stripe together); a code's fixed amount and a shipping rate are converted, and a booking's price is worked out in the country's own currency first and then converted once. The header (and the phone's menu and the footer) offers a language and a currency choice (`ViewMenu`, `src/components/view-menu.tsx`, links to the same page in the other address), the canonical address is always the country's own view, and every language of every country is a `hreflang` alternate (sitemap, page head, Open Graph `alternateLocale`). Owners set it at `/admin/{store}/settings/localization` (owners change it, staff see it): languages with the main one and each country's default, currencies with rate and rounding, and "Keep the rates up to date" (`rates_auto`), which the daily cron (`refreshAutoRates()`, from `src/lib/ecb.ts`'s parser of the ECB's daily XML) applies to stores that ask, or "Fetch the ECB's rates now". Anything that was `store.markets.map(m => m.locale)` (translations, menus, product texts, alt texts, chat knowledge, SEO texts) now reads `store.localization.locales`, so a store can write and translate in English without selling to an English-speaking country. In the page builder's right sidebar *Translate with AI* (`TranslateWithAi` in `page-editor.tsx`, `src/lib/page-translate-ai.ts`, `src/server/page-translate.ts`, `translateStorePageAction`, feature `page_translation` in the usage reports) translates the page as it stands in the editor, saved or not, into one other language or all of them: only the texts not translated yet, or everything again over the translations there are. Rich text is sent as its runs of formatting and put back into the same document, so bold and links stay where they were. The model gets the page's words (with what each is, and its longest length) and no prices or stock; each answer is checked for its shape, its length and the claims filter (`findClaims()`: a translation with more claims, prices or urgency than the source is left out, and the text stays in the main language, which the panel says), and the result is only put into the page as a draft translation for staff to read, change and save. Emails about an order link back to the language and currency it was placed in while the store still offers them (`context()` in `src/server/shopper-emails.ts`), else to the country's own address. No place falls back to a currency of its own: a store's main currency is its own country's (`mainCurrency()`), Kaizen's plan currencies list its own country's first, and a store with no country yet gets euro or a clear message. Not yet: a rate per market's own currency entered by hand for a single product, and other decimal separators than the language's own in the admin's amount fields. *Since D178 step 4 (`docs/store-features.md` 4d):* other languages and currencies than a country's own are offered only while Several languages and Several currencies are on (`Localization.languageChoice`/`currencyChoice`; with Several currencies off a country is not shown in another country's own currency either); an address asking for one not offered moves (308) to the same page in the country's own address, and what was bought keeps opening as it was (`resolveAfterSaleShop()`); the rates stay for history. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: language and currency must be separate choices, with a store owner offering various currencies whatever the language and various languages whatever the currencies, plus a *Translate with AI* in the page builder. The choice lives in the address so each view is cacheable and indexable, needs no cookie (`KNOWN_COOKIES` is unchanged), and the old addresses stay as they were. | D111 | **The platform chooses the languages stores can offer, and the interface text of new languages is translated by AI into data, read by a person and used key by key.** *Registry* (`commerce.platform_languages`, `src/server/languages.ts`, `/admin/platform/languages`): each language has its code, its locales (the main one first, then variants such as `de-AT`), its English name, a switch and a direction; it starts with every language stores could already be in (D109) and a platform admin adds more from the world's languages (`src/lib/languages.ts`: a curated list of about 85 with names from `Intl`, or any ISO code `Intl` knows). A store picks its languages among the enabled ones at `/admin/{store}/settings/localization`, which shows where each one's interface text stands; a language stores use, and English (everything falls back to it), cannot be switched off. Products, pages, menus, alt texts and the AI translations of D109–D110 already worked in any language; this adds the *interface*: buttons, cart, checkout, account pages and the shoppers' emails (`src/lib/i18n.ts`, `src/lib/email-text.ts`), written by hand in Norwegian, Swedish, Danish and English and never in the database. *Catalogue* (`src/lib/ui-catalog.ts`): the English messages as entries with a key (`ui:cart.title`, `email:orderSubject`) and a text, or, for a message that takes arguments (about 170, of which 28 choose by a number or a yes or no, `CHOOSING`), a template in a small ICU subset (`src/lib/icu-lite.ts`: `{0}`, `{0, plural, one {# day} other {# days}}` with the language's own plural forms from `Intl.PluralRules`, `{1, select, true {…} other {…}}`); a test holds every template to the English function's output for arguments of each kind, so a new English message is found by the catalogue at once and one that chooses must be added to `CHOOSING` with its case in `ui-catalog.test.ts`. *Texts* (`commerce.ui_translations`: language, key, text, a fingerprint of the English it was translated from, `ai` or `staff`, and when a person read it): the platform admin's page for a language (`/admin/platform/languages/{lang}`) plans what is missing, out of date (English changed) or everything the AI wrote, sends it in chunks of 40 to Kaizen's own AI (`aiFor(null, { feature: 'ui_translation' })`, never a store's key), and keeps only what passes the checks (`src/lib/ui-translate.ts`: every placeholder kept and no new one, named placeholders such as `{left}` kept, a select's options kept, a plural in the language's own forms, plain text, not far longer than the English); what fails is listed and left for a person. A person edits a group of texts (checked the same way, kept as `staff` and reviewed, never replaced by the AI, not even by translating everything again), marks texts reviewed, or removes a language's texts to start again. *Reading* (`src/server/ui-text.ts`, `src/lib/ui-registry.ts`): `t(lang)` and `emailText(lang)` stay synchronous; for a language without hand-written text they read a registry on `globalThis` of `Messages` built from the texts (`overlayMessages()`: each text or template replaces the English one, a template becoming a function of the same arguments, and anything the language lacks stays English, key by key rather than the whole language). A server instance reads the texts when it starts (`src/instrumentation.ts`, `startUi()`) and looks for changes each minute; pages never wait on the database for text, and it is not read while a page is prerendered (which would break `cacheComponents`), so a page cached before a language was translated shows English until it is drawn again. The storefront layout hands the language's texts to the browser (`UiTexts`, which registers them so client components' `t(lang)` speak it). Not yet: right-to-left layout (the registry keeps each language's direction, but nothing sets `dir` or mirrors the layout), fonts in scripts beyond Latin, Greek and Cyrillic, keyword-search stemming beyond the twelve languages Postgres has (`commerce.search_config()`; others match without stemming), and legal texts, which stay out of the catalogue and need a person's translation. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: expand multilingual support beyond Norwegian, Swedish and Danish with any language chosen from a full list in the platform admin, translated to it. The content layer was already language-open; the interface was the gap (English for every other language), and moving it to data lets Kaizen add a language without a deploy while keeping the hand-written languages as they are. Templates with plural and select, checked against the language's own plural forms, are what let an AI translate messages that are functions in code without breaking numbers, names or gender-like choices; keeping every text a person writes and a fingerprint of its English makes re-translation safe and a changed original noticeable. Translating the interface is a platform job (once, for every store), reviewed once, rather than each store paying for it. |
| D112 | **A store's blog, search page and 404 page are pages built in the page builder, like its front page and All products page.** `commerce.page_roles` (store, role, page; `src/lib/page-roles.ts`, `pageForRole()` and `setPageRole()` in `src/server/pages.ts`, `store.pageRoles`) says which of a store's published pages is its *blog* (`/blog`, `PAGE_ROLES` `blog`), its *search page* (`/search`, `search`) or the page shown when an address is not found (`not_found`); the site's standard page shows until one is chosen or while the chosen one is unpublished, and deleting the page lets the role go (the database cascades). A page has one place: not the front page, the All products page or another role's (the database keeps the store's own pages, one place each). Owners choose them on the Pages list under the front page and All products forms (`PageRoleForm`), where *Start from a new page* makes a published starter in the store's main language that looks like the standard page (`starterPage()`: a heading and a grid of articles; a heading and the search; a heading, a search box and a button to the store), in place and opened in the builder (`createRolePage()`). The pages' own addresses lead to their places (the blog and search pages redirect permanently, the 404 page answers 404) and they are left out of the sitemap and llms.txt as the front page is. The 404 page is drawn by the market's `not-found.tsx`, so it is a real 404 with the store's header and footer. New stores get copies of the template's (`clone_store()`). A new *Search* component (`SearchBlock`, `SearchSection` in `src/components/search-section.tsx`, in the palette of a store's pages and articles) draws the store's search box and, unless its `results` is off, its results as the standard page does (query understanding, meaning search, filters, click and zero-result logging all as before; it reads the address through `GridPlace.listing`, so a page that holds it searches at its own path); with only the box it leads to the search page, which is what a 404 page or a header wants. Search belongs in a store's pages and articles (`pageRulesProblem()`). Blog categories and tags at `/blog/category|tag/…` keep the standard list. Not yet, and the places to take next with the same mechanism: category and tag pages (they need a grid that follows the term in the address), the country chooser at `/s/{store}`, the cart, checkout and order confirmation, My account and its sign-in, the wishlist, the subscription and delivery pages, the cookies page, and Kaizen's own site (its front page, blog, sign-up, cookies and 404 are still written in code, and platform pages have no roles). | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: the blog archive, 404 and search pages should be builder pages like All products and the front page, and which others had been missed. One table for the roles rather than a column each keeps the next places one line each (a role, its route, a starter); a Search component rather than a search-only page type keeps the builder's blocks the only way a page is made and lets a 404 page or a landing page hold the search too. |
| D113 | **A store's working pages are page-builder pages too: cart, checkout, order confirmation, My account, sign-in, wishlists, a subscription, weekly deliveries and cookies.** Each is a role in `commerce.page_roles` (D112; `PAGE_ROLES` now also `cart`, `checkout`, `order`, `account`, `sign_in`, `wishlist`, `subscription`, `deliveries`, `cookies`, the check constraint widened by a migration) chosen on the Pages list, where the special pages are now grouped (`ROLE_GROUPS`: content pages, shopping, customer account, information). A working page holds one *shop component* (`StorePartBlock`, `type: "storePart"`, `part` one of `STORE_PARTS` in `src/lib/store-parts.ts`; in the palette of a store's pages, not articles, headers, footers or Kaizen's: `storePartProblem()`), drawn by `StorePartSection` from the same section components the standard pages are made of (`CartContents`, `Checkout`, `OrderDetails`, `AccountSection`/`SignInForm`, `WishlistSection`, `SubscriptionSection`, `DeliveriesSection`, `CookiesSection`, each moved out of its route's `page.tsx` into a `*-section.tsx` beside it). A component draws only on the route it belongs to: the route hands the page `GridPlace.route` (`StoreRoute`: the part, the address's own part such as an order's id or a subscription link's secret, and the query) through `RolePage` (`src/components/role-page.tsx`, the chosen page or the standard page as its children), and a component whose part is not the route's draws nothing, so a cart component on the About page does no harm and checkout never redirects from another page. Components that depend on the shopper's state bring their own heading (order, account, wishlists, subscription, deliveries, cookies); the cart, checkout and sign-in starters have a heading block of their own (`starterPage()`). Signed out, My account shows the sign-in page where one is chosen (`sign_in`, drawn at `/account`), else the account page's own component, which is the sign-in form; emailed sign-in links, company pages, order details under My account, downloads and unsubscribe links keep their standard pages. A page's own address leads to its route (permanent redirect) or, where the route has none (order, subscription, 404), answers 404; the pages are left out of the sitemap as D112's are. The cart page is built this way but phones still get the slide-out cart (D64). Cookies keep the component so the list stays reachable. Not yet: category and tag pages, the country chooser, Kaizen's own site. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: the cart, checkout, order confirmation, My account, sign-in, wishlist, subscription, delivery and cookies pages should be builder pages too. One generic component with a part, rather than a block per page, and the route-scoped drawing keep the working pages' behaviour (redirects, per-request data, secrets in addresses) in one place each while the words, layout and extra content around them are the owner's. |
| D114 | **Stores run campaigns: offers without a code, for a time.** A campaign (`commerce.campaigns`, `src/lib/campaigns.ts`, `src/server/campaigns.ts`; `/admin/{store}/campaigns` under Sales) is one of three kinds: *a percentage off*; *buy N, pay for M* (3 for 2: in every full group of N units, over all the lines it reaches together, the N − M cheapest are free); or *a free product* added to the order when the basket comes to an amount per market (in the country's own currency, converted when read like a code's minimum, D109). Each can be for the whole store or for chosen products and categories or tags (`product_ids`, `term_ids`; a category takes in its subcategories through `termsOfProducts()`; for a gift the choice is what counts towards the amount), and has an optional start and end (Norwegian time) and a switch. Campaigns take a reduction off *goods bought once* (not appointments, stays, rentals or subscriptions), first: the buyer's group discount (D108) then comes off what is left (`memberOffAfterCampaign()`), then any code (D31; a percentage code on a reduced line comes off what is left, `applyDiscount()`). A unit gets at most one campaign: the campaign giving most on the lines still free wins, ties to the older (`applyCampaigns()`). A free product is only goods the store ships itself (`giftItems()`: physical, kind goods, no host, not subscription-only, active, priced in the market, in stock for the whole quantity), and only when the order ships anyway and has no host's listing; it is a line of the order at its list price with all of it taken off (`order_lines.gift`, `campaign_discount_minor`, `campaign_id`), so it is held, shipped, invoiced and shown like any other line, and shown as struck through and "Gratis" in the cart, checkout, order pages and emails. `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()` both use `evaluateCampaigns()` and agree to the øre (`checkout-kinds.int.test.ts` has a scenario for each kind, with a group, a company, a code and euro); the order keeps the campaigns' part (`orders.campaign_discount_minor`, `campaign_label`), `discountParts()`/`discountNote()` split a discount into campaign, group and code, and Stripe takes it all in the same one-time coupon, named after them. A gift line is not part of the cart, so the checkout's "cart changed" check leaves it out. Changing or deleting a campaign never changes an order. New stores get none (`clone_store()` does not copy them). Not yet: a notice on product pages and cards (they are cached, and a time-limited notice needs a run-time read), campaigns for a customer group only, a limit on uses, stacking two campaigns on one unit, free shipping and "spend X get Y off" as kinds, and the AI manager's tools for campaigns. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: scheduled and time-limited campaigns for single products or by category/tag, a free product above a basket amount, and 3 for 2. Taking campaigns off before the group's discount and codes, from goods bought once, keeps the cart, checkout and Stripe agreeing and leaves subscriptions' and bookings' own pricing as it is. The free product being an ordinary order line at a full discount, rather than a special case, keeps stock, shipping, invoices and returns working unchanged; giving only goods the store ships, and only to orders that ship, avoids consent for downloads, shipping added by a gift and a gift from a host's stock. |
| D115 | **Campaigns are announced on product pages and cards without slowing them, can be limited to customer groups and to a number of orders, can stack, and the AI manager runs them.** *Notices* (`src/lib/campaign-notices.ts`, `src/server/campaign-notices.ts`): `campaignNotices(storeId, market)` is a `'use cache'` read tagged `campaigns:{storeId}` (`campaignsTag`), so a product page or card gets its campaigns like it gets the product: from the cache, no read per request, in the prerendered shell (a new `campaigns` product part, in the default layout after the price and for owners' own layouts to add; a badge of the offer on the price, two at most, on product cards, in `ProductGrid` and content grids; a free product is told on the product's page only). The cache lasts to the next start or end of a campaign, at least a minute and at most an hour, with the expiry kept past five minutes (a shorter one would make the notice a dynamic hole, out of the prerendered page), and a minute while a campaign has a limit; the admin's save, delete and switch refresh the tag (`updateTag` in the actions, `ctx.invalidate` in the AI tools). `CampaignEnds` (a client component on `useSyncExternalStore`) hides an announcement the moment its campaign ends, so the cached page never promises what the cart no longer gives. Only what is the same for everyone is announced: never a campaign for chosen customer groups, and a limited one only while it has orders left. Measured locally on the production build (curl, 40 requests each, median): product page, All products, front page and category page answer in ~4 ms to first byte and ~21–31 ms in all, the same with three campaigns running as without any; the first visit to a page not prerendered (which reads the notices once) took 100–240 ms. *Limits*: `campaigns.usage_limit` (orders in all: an order counts once however many lines got it, pending payment counted and a cancelled checkout giving its use back, as a code's; `placeOrder()` locks the store's limited campaigns while it counts, so two checkouts cannot take the last use), `tier_ids` (only customers in those customer groups, their company's group included, D108: `customerTierIds()`; needs a signed-in customer), and `stacks` (a percentage that, instead of competing, comes off what the campaign that won left, one after another in the order they were made, `applyCampaigns()`; only for percentages). Each order line keeps every campaign that gave on it (`order_lines.campaign_parts`, `[{ id, name, minor }]`, the first being `campaign_id`), which the list's orders and given figures read. *The AI manager*: `list_campaigns`, and `create_campaign` and `set_campaign_active` gated as `public` (kept for the owner's yes, with an `approvalSummary()`); a campaign is made from words (products by title or handle, categories, tags and customer groups by name, days as 2026-10-01, a free product by SKU, its amount in the main country's currency), and served to Kaizen Life's MCP with the other owner tools; the "run a sale" playbook names them. Not yet: a limit per customer, campaigns for a country only, and stacking a 3 for 2 with a second 3 for 2. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: the notice on product pages and cards, keeping pages under half a second to load; tools for the AI manager; limits to customer groups and a number of uses; and stacking. Reading campaigns through the catalogue's own cache, with a lifetime that follows the campaigns' dates and the page ending an announcement itself, is what keeps the shell prerendered and the time to first byte where it was; leaving group campaigns out of the notices keeps a cached page from saying what only some can have. |
| D116 | **Campaigns can be limited per customer and to countries, a "buy N pay for M" can stack, and the chat agent shows their offers.** *Per customer* (`campaigns.per_customer_limit`, 1 for once per customer): counted from the customer's own non-cancelled orders through `order_lines.campaign_parts`, only for a signed-in customer (`runningCampaigns()` gives none to someone who is not, since it cannot tell who has had it), locked in `placeOrder()` with the limited campaigns; the product pages still announce it, with "Sign in to My account to get it" (`CampaignNotice.signIn`). *Countries* (`campaigns.markets`, market codes; none, or all of the store's, is every country): `runsIn()` in the engine's caller and in the notices; a free product still needs its amount for each country it runs in. *Stacking a "buy N pay for M"*: a stacking one works over the units still to be paid for after the campaign that won and the stackers before it, each unit free at most once (`freed` per line in `applyCampaigns()`; a line's remaining value is spread evenly over the units left to pay for, and the cheapest of every full group of N are free), so two 3 for 2 on six items free three, not two; percentages and multi-buys stack in the order the campaigns were made, and a free product cannot stack (the database keeps `stacks` to percentages and multi-buys). *Chat* (`ChatProduct.offers`, `offersOn()` in `src/server/chat-agent.ts`): cards the agent draws carry the offer badges (two at most, never a gift, as on the storefront's cards), and the `search_products` and `get_product` results give the model the offers as the site words them (`Name: 20 % rabatt`, a gift and its sign-in hint on a product's own page, and "taken off in the cart"), so it states no offer of its own; group campaigns are told to no one. Not yet: a limit per customer over a period, and a stacking free product. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner as the remaining follow-ups to D114 and D115. Counting a customer's orders from the same parts that count a campaign's uses, and locking the same rows, keeps one rule for both limits; announcing a per-customer campaign with a sign-in hint, rather than hiding it, tells shoppers what they must do to get what the page says. |
| D117 | **The cart, checkout and order pages come apart in the page builder, as a product's page does.** The whole components (`cart`, `checkout`, `order`) stay for pages that want them; beside them are pieces (`STORE_PIECES` in `src/lib/store-parts.ts`), each a `storePart` block whose `part` names it and whose page is `routeOfPart()`: *cart* items, summary, discount code, checkout button and a way back to the store; *checkout* items, discount code, totals, payment form and a link back to the cart; *order* thank-you (also refreshing the page while the payment is confirmed), account created, bookings, items, totals, subscription, downloads, delivery address and a way back to the store. A piece draws only on its own page (`StorePartSection`), and a piece with nothing to show (a summary of an empty cart, downloads of an order with none) draws nothing. Pieces are bare (no frame or heading): the block's spacing, border and shadow settings make them look as the owner likes, and the whole page frames them itself. The pieces of one page read the cart, the checkout or the order once per request (`perRequest()` in `src/server/request-memo.ts`, so the order is not fetched, nor Stripe asked, for each piece) and draw the same functions the whole component does, so the two cannot drift. Starter pages for the cart, checkout and order are made of pieces laid out as the standard pages are (`starterPage()`); pages already built with a whole component keep working. Roles stay the whole pages (`PAGE_ROLES` follows `STORE_PARTS`, not the pieces). No migration: a piece is a value in the page's JSON. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: to rearrange these pages in creative ways, and a working page in one piece could only be moved as a whole. Splitting it the same way as the product page (D79) gives the same freedom without a second mechanism: same block, same builder palette, same page rules. A separate block type per page was rejected as three copies of the same plumbing; making pieces roles would have offered "a page for the cart summary". |
| D118 | **Owners define their own custom fields, as in WordPress's Advanced Custom Fields, and place them in product layouts and pages.** Groups of fields are the store's (`commerce.field_groups`: name, web name, the kinds of thing they can be on, OR-of-AND location rules over a product's kind, audience, categories (with their parents) and tags, or a page's categories, tags and special page, the fields as one jsonb array, main or side column, on/off); what was entered is `commerce.field_values`, one row per product, page or article and language (`locale` empty for what is the same in every language), keyed by the field's immutable `id` so a field can be renamed without losing its values, taken away with the thing by triggers. The pure part is `src/lib/custom-fields.ts` (types, `fieldGroupInput`, location rules, conditional logic in ACF's shape (a field is shown by fields above it in the group; a hidden field is kept but never required), `parseFieldChanges`, presets, export and import), the server `src/server/custom-fields.ts`, the generator `/admin/{store}/fields` (`FieldGroupEditor`), the entry form `FieldsForm`/`EntityFields` in the product and page editors (values travel in the same JSON and the same transaction, checked against the groups the finished thing gets). Field types: text, text area, number, measurement, email, web address, phone, select, radio, button group, checkboxes, yes/no, rich text (Tiptap JSON, never HTML), picture, gallery, video (YouTube/Vimeo), date, date and time, time, colour. Only texts are per language (`isTranslatable()`), and the main language's text shows where another has none. A field is **private until the owner makes it public** (`access`), and the site shows only public ones (`shownFieldsFor()`, cached under `fieldsTag`, read inside the product page's own cached read so the page stays prerendered). Product layouts get `fields` and `field` parts and pages a `customField` block; the standard product page shows public groups after the description. `clone_store()` copies groups (rules following the copied categories and tags) and values; `products.attributes`, which nothing used, was dropped. What phase 2 added is in D119. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner, after research into ACF (`docs/custom-fields.md`). Values by id and one row per thing and language avoid ACF's orphaned values after a rename and its two-meta-rows-per-value bloat; placing fields visually in the builder needs no theme code; private by default follows Shopify's metafields, as a shop's owners keep things they would not want on the site. |
| D119 | **Custom fields grow: groups and repeaters, links, relations and files, fields on variants and on categories and tags, binding, filters, search, structured data, chat, translation and the AI manager.** A group and a repeater hold sub fields (structure and non-text cells shared, translatable texts per language, repeater rows with stable ids so a translation follows its row); links point at a page, product, category, tag or web address; product, page and category or tag relations and files (own bucket `field-files`, uploaded from the browser) hold ids and addresses that the server checks belong to the store and resolves on read, leaving out what is gone. Variants have fields by SKU in the product editor, drawn per variant on the product page as the picker changes, and categories and tags in their manager and on their page (`field_values.entity` widened, triggers, `clone_store()`). A public field can be a listing filter (`f.<name>`), part of keyword search (`commerce.field_search`, one row per product and language, stemmed like titles), a fact for the chat agent and, for simple types, a structured-data property. A heading, rich text, image or button can take its content from a field (`bind`, resolved before drawing). Field texts are in the store translation worklist and the AI manager reads, fills and makes groups (`set_fields` and `create_field_group` kept for the owner's yes). Left for phase 3: the store and customers as things, money, loop and flexible content, listing tile bindings, number ranges as filters, variants and categories in search. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for ("Go ahead with phase 2"), following `docs/custom-fields.md`. Ids and shared rows keep translations and values through renames and reordering; relations stored as ids never leak another store's names; filters, search and bindings only read public fields, so making a field public is the one decision an owner makes about what a shopper can see. |
| D120 | **Custom fields, phase 3: money, flexible content, the store, customers and orders as things with fields, a loop block, field values on grid tiles, and range filters.** A money field is an amount in a currency the store offers, shown in the market's currency at the store's rates (its own when no rate is known), never as a price: no VAT, no chat, no structured data. Flexible content is a list of rows of owner-defined layouts, stored like a repeater's (shared structure, words by row id). The store has one set of values for site-wide facts, shown through `source: 'store'` on the fields components and on bindings, also in headers and footers. Customer and order fields are staff-only by rule (own groups, no rules, nothing public, no files or pictures), never read on the shopper's side (a test lists every reader of the values), and a customer's go with the customer. The loop block draws a repeater's rows from slots (not a nested builder); a content grid can show up to three plain fields on its tiles, read in one batch; number and measurement fields filter as ranges. Left: money in bindings, customer and order fields in exports beyond `customerFieldExport()` (nothing calls it yet), guests' fields (values are by customer account), a measurement range across units. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for ("Go ahead with phase 3"), following `docs/custom-fields.md`. Slots instead of a nested builder keep the loop simple and safe to render; the readers test keeps personal data out of every public path by construction rather than by review. |
| D121 | **A row can be a modal.** A row with a `modal` setting (`RowModal`, `src/lib/page-modal.ts`) is taken out of the page's flow and drawn in a native `<dialog>` by `PageModal`, opened by a link to `#modal-{key}` (a button, a menu link or text), any element with a chosen class, a timer or exit intent (on touch screens a quick scroll up). Its content is an ordinary row, so every block, form and video works inside; in a footer or header it is site-wide. Auto triggers open at most once per page view, never over another modal, never on working pages or in the builder; it always closes with Escape and a visible control. Frequency (always, once per session, every N days) is kept in browser storage as a `preferences` item (`kaizen_modal_{key}`), only for visitors who allowed preferences. A modal's words are not in the page's description or main heading, but stay in the chat's knowledge. | 2026-09-29 | Asked for by the owner: one multi-purpose modal for the page builder with four triggers. A row setting reuses the whole builder for content instead of a nested one, and the native dialog gives focus handling and Escape for free. |
| D122 | **Work: clients, assignments, time and invoices in the store admin, ported from Kaizen Life's Work area (`docs/work.md`), so consultants sell and manage hours in one system.** A module switched on under Features (`stores.modules` `work`), at `/admin/{store}/work`: clients (archived, never deleted with history), assignments with tasks and estimates, time entries and one running timer per person (the SQL functions start, stop and log, minutes rounded up and capped at a day, dated in the store's time zone), estimate alerts, invoices, and an overview. Money is integer minor units and hours integer minutes (quantities in hundredths of an hour), never floats. **Invoices are legal documents**: a draft becomes an invoice only through `commerce.issue_work_invoice()`, which numbers it gap-free from its own series (`W-`, credits `WCN-`) at issue, checks readiness (seller and buyer details, bank account, VAT treatment), freezes seller, buyer, lines, VAT and totals as a snapshot, and from then on nothing about it can change; mistakes are corrected by credit notes (`credit_work_invoice()`, whole or by line, releasing the time), payments are append-only and the status follows them by trigger. One draft at a time per assignment. VAT per line from a treatment the owner chooses (domestic, not registered, reverse charge, outside scope, exempt) with hand-written notes in nb, sv, da and en (`src/lib/work-invoice-text.ts`, kept out of the AI catalogue, **needs legal and accountant review before real use**). The printable invoice and credit note come from the snapshot at `/admin/{store}/work/invoices/{id}/print`. Left out of Life on purpose: Finances links, organizations, agent personas. Not yet: emailing invoices and a hosted page for clients, recurring invoices and their cron, reports and CSV, linking clients to store customers, selling hours as products with a prepaid ledger, online payment, owner tools and events. | 2026-09-30 | Asked for by the owner: "clone all of its functionality" from Life. Life's own send only marked an invoice sent, numbers were typed by hand and the print was the browser's; Kaizen's rules (legal numbering, immutability, VAT, store isolation) replace those, so this is a port of the behaviour, not of the code. |
| D123 | **Work lives at the store owner's level (the control center), across all their stores.** One place for every income stream: `/admin/account/work` shows the combined overview, clients, time, invoices, reports and settings of every store the account belongs to with Work on; one store's own screens are under `/admin/account/work/s/{store}/…` (`src/lib/work-paths.ts`: `workBase()`, the only way to build a Work link). The data stays per store, because each store is the legal seller with its own invoice series, VAT, bank details and currency; the combined view groups money per currency and never adds across them, and every read takes only the stores the account's memberships allow (`src/server/work-owner.ts`). The Work switch moved from Features to the owner's Work settings (owners only, audited). A person has one running timer across all stores (`work_start_timer` stops one anywhere; `getRunningTimer(accountId)`; unique per account). The store level lost its Work tab and sidebar group; old `/admin/{store}/work…` addresses redirect (`LEGACY_WORK_REDIRECTS`). Print routes stay chrome-free under `(print)/account/work/s/{store}`. |
| D124 | **A Work client can be filled in from Brønnøysundregistrene's open Enhetsregisteret.** Type a Norwegian organisation number or a company name on the client form (`BrregLookup`); the register (`data.brreg.no`, NLOD, no key) gives the registered name, business address (else the postal address) and whether the company is in the VAT register, and the form fills in the legal name, organisation number, address, country and, only for a company registered for VAT, its VAT number `NO{number}MVA`. Pure parts in `src/lib/brreg.ts` (the mod-11 check digit is checked before any call; a name in capitals is read as a name; a bankrupt, winding-up or deleted company gets a warning, not a refusal), calls in `src/server/brreg.ts` (fixed host, 6 s timeout, a lookup cached a day, a search not cached, a failing register is `unavailable` and never blocks saving), actions `lookupCompanyAction`/`searchCompaniesAction` behind `requireMember` and the Work switch. Only the number or the typed words are sent. The register has no bank account, email or phone, so those stay manual; the managing director and chair are not fetched (personal data, and invoices go to the company). Everything filled in stays editable. The same panel (`src/components/admin/brreg-lookup.tsx`, taking its two actions as props so each place keeps its own rule about who may ask) is on a shop's company accounts (`CompanyFields`, `/admin/{store}/companies`): there it fills only the name (if none is typed) and the organisation number as digits, since a company account holds no address or VAT number; its actions (`lookupRegisterCompanyAction`, `searchRegisterCompaniesAction`) ask only for store membership, not for Work. |
| D125 | **Saved rows, columns and components can be shared as templates.** The builder's left sidebar has a Templates tab (replacing Layers) showing the templates the store has switched on, from its owner's other stores or the marketplace, grouped as rows, columns and components; "Browse templates" opens a modal with My stores and Marketplace tabs, kind chips, an "activated only" filter and search, and each template switches on or off. Saving a part asks who may use it: `commerce.saved_parts.sharing` is `private` (this store, the default), `stores` (other stores the same account **owns**) or `marketplace` (every store owner, live at once); only owners choose. Kaizen's own saved parts are the marketplace's Kaizen listing, on by default until a store switches them off (`template_activations`, `coalesce(active, store_id is null)`). Using a template is a copy (`applyTemplate()`): `sanitizeTemplate()` strips the source store's links, ids, form recipients, menus, field bindings, personal links and another owner's HTML, and pictures are copied in Storage into the using store's media library (at most 30 files, 80 MB); nothing keeps pointing at the publisher. Visibility is one SQL condition read on every request (a lost ownership takes a `stores` template away at once). Platform admins hide or show marketplace templates at `/admin/platform/templates`. | D126 | **A page can be duplicated** from the pages list and from the builder's toolbar (pages, articles, product layouts, headers and footers, Kaizen's and stores'). From the builder it copies what the editor holds now, unsaved changes too, without `globalEdits` (a copy never changes a global part for the pages using it) or field entries; a page's or article's custom field values are copied by SQL. Audited as `{store\|platform}.{type}_duplicated`. |
| D127 | **Whole page layouts are templates too, and any template can be previewed before it is switched on or used.** A page layout (`saved_parts.kind = 'page'`, content `PageLayout`: the kind of page it is for, its rows and the page's own CSS, `src/lib/page-layout.ts`) is saved from the builder toolbar (Save as template), listed under Saved, and shared, switched on, hidden and moderated exactly like a row, column or component (D125); never global, and none of the page's own settings (title, address, search texts, categories, translations, a header's overlay) come with it. It is only for pages of its own kind (`layoutFits()`: a header's rows hold components no article has). Using one asks whether to **replace** the page's rows or **add** after them (`applyPageLayout()`, fresh ids, unique custom ids, row and block caps; the layout's CSS is used when the page has none, or replaces the page's on replace only if the layout has some); the builder has no undo, and the dialog says so. **Preview** (`previewTemplate()`, `/admin/account/templates/s/{store}/{id}/preview`, chrome-free in the `(print)` group) draws a template as the store's own pages are (its theme, fonts and CSS; header and footer with the store's details, a product layout with one of its products, an article under its heading) in an inert frame the builder shows at desktop, tablet or phone width; it is view-only (nothing copied, saved or audited; pictures are still the publisher's) and follows the same `visibleTo()` rule as using. Kaizen's own builder has none of the browsing or preview, its saved page layouts being the marketplace's. |
| D128 | **Motion design: curated effects on components, rows, columns and backgrounds, and a "Make my page cool" button.** Owners pick from lists (`src/lib/motion.ts`: 20 entrances, 10 hover effects, 9 scroll effects, 6 background effects, animated gradient backgrounds `shift`/`aurora`/`mesh`/`conic` with optional grain) in a Motion tab of every settings dialog and under a background; only names and small named amounts are stored (`PartBase.motion`, row/column `backgroundMotion`, schemas shared with the server), never CSS or code. It is CSS first (`src/app/motion.css`: keyframes, `animation-timeline: view()` for scroll effects, hover) with a small lazy runtime (`src/lib/motion-runtime.ts`, the `motion` package's `scroll()` only for browsers without scroll timelines, about 3 kB gzip) for waypoint entrances (`inView`-style, `start` early/middle/late, `once`), text splitting, tilt/magnetic/spotlight and the Firefox fallback; it loads only where a page needs it (`pageUsesMotion()`). Rules: content is always in the HTML and shown without JavaScript, with `prefers-reduced-motion`, and after a 4 s failsafe; `load` entrances and the first flow row are pure CSS and never wait for JS; a first-row picture never starts transparent (LCP); only `transform`, `opacity`, `filter` and `clip-path` move, so nothing shifts the layout. `partFx()`/`backgroundFx()` turn the data into `data-fx-*` attributes and `--fx-*` custom properties for the site and the builder's canvas ("Preview motion", live preview in the tab). **Make my page cool** (wand button in the builder toolbar, `MakeCoolDialog`, `planMotionAction`, `planPageMotion()` in `src/server/motion-ai.ts`, feature `page_motion`): the site's AI gets a compact outline of the page (structure and headings' words, no prices, emails, links or form data) and may only choose effect and part ids; `cleanMotionPlan()` enforces one coherent style, caps (about one part in three, at most two entrance families), the first row's rules and no nesting, and the summary is written in code; without AI, or if its answer is unusable, `ruleBasedPlan()` gives a standard design. `applyMotionPlan()` never changes motion the owner already set, the result lands in the editor unsaved with one Undo, and the canvas plays it. No database change (motion is part of a page's content). |
| D129 | **An owner can duplicate one of their stores** (`/admin/stores` → Duplicate, wizard at `/admin/stores/copy/{store}`, progress at `/admin/stores/copies/{id}`; `docs/store-copy.md`). The owner chooses pages, products and posts (all, selected or none), customers (shoppers only) and order history; a store's settings always come except secrets, and whatever only makes sense for the original never does (payment connection and keys, domains, integrations, AI keys, invoices and numbering, billing and plan, the team, Work, logs, consents, carts, and customers' subscriptions and subscription boxes; a product's subscription plans come with the product). The content step is one transaction, `commerce.duplicate_store()` (deterministic `clone_id()` mapping; references to things not copied are dropped, scoped campaigns and codes switched off; tracking ids and the store's own code are not copied), then a resumable job (`store_copies`, run by the five-minute cron: pictures and files into the new store's own library and every address rewritten, never left pointing at the original; customers and orders in batches). Customers come without passwords, sessions, carts, wish lists or consents, and opt-outs stay opted out. Orders are read-only history: numbered `C-{original}`, marked by `copied_from`, status kept, and structurally unable to be paid, refunded, shipped, invoiced, emailed, booked or to reserve stock (triggers), skipped by revenue, analytics, control center, cron and integration events. A data-use confirmation is required with customers or orders. The new store is owned by the requester only, starts closed with no Stripe account, and `COPY_RULES` (`src/lib/store-copy-rules.ts`) classifies every store-owned table, with a test that fails when a new one is not decided. |
| D130 | **A bonus program: credits for a store's customers** (`/admin/{store}/bonus` under Sales; `docs/bonus.md`; contract `src/lib/bonus.ts`, engine `src/server/bonus.ts`, rules in SQL `commerce.bonus_*`). A signed-in customer earns credits, money in the store's main currency (pinned in `bonus_settings.currency`, shown converted in other markets), as a percentage (5 % by default) of what was paid online for goods (VAT included; not shipping, the credits used or a venue's balance); they are pending until the return period ends (14 days), then usable as a price reduction on goods **last**, after campaigns, group discounts and codes, at most `maxRedeemPercent` of the goods (default 50, max 90), never leaving less than the payment provider's minimum charge. Expiry is owner-chosen and off by default (oldest first, one reminder email 14 days ahead). The ledger is append-only (`bonus_entries`, lots taken from through `bonus_allocations`, an idempotency key on every entry); balance is worked out from it and `bonus_verify()` checks the invariant. Orders call in through triggers (paid, cancelled, refunds), so every path that pays, cancels or refunds is covered: a refund takes back the refunded share of what the order earned (never below zero) and returns the same share of what it used. Used credits are a discount (`orders.credit_minor` is part of `discount_minor`, line by line in `order_lines.bonus_discount_minor`, so VAT and refunds agree). Copied orders (D129) never earn; a store copy carries the settings, never the balances. Owner tools `get_bonus_program`, `set_bonus_program` (gate `public`) and `adjust_customer_credits` (gate `spend`). Known limits: refunds made in Stripe's dashboard do not move credits; subscription renewals cannot redeem; no tiered rates or transfers. |
| D131 | **An affiliate program on two levels** (`docs/referrals.md`, `docs/affiliates.md`). **Platform** (`src/lib/referrals.ts`, `src/server/referrals.ts`, `src/server/referral-billing.ts`, SQL `commerce.referral_*`): any store owner gets a code and link at `/admin/account/referrals` (`/r/{code}` → `/sign-up?ref=`), a platform admin switches the program on and sets the rate at `/admin/platform/referrals`. A referred store's plan invoices (amount without VAT, Stripe `invoice.paid`) and Kaizen's sale fee on its orders earn the referrer `commissionBps` (10 %) for `months` (12) from when the referral was made; it is **credit, per currency and never converted**, pending 30 days, then put on the referrer's own Kaizen plan invoices as one negative invoice line when Stripe says `invoice.created` (ledger entry first, Stripe line idempotent, given back if the line cannot be made or the invoice is voided); refunds and credit notes take back their share, never below zero. The ledger is append-only like the bonus ledger (lots, allocations, idempotency keys, advisory lock per account). Self-referral (same email, or a member of the new store) and blocked accounts earn nothing; a referrer sees referred stores by name and money only. **Store** (`src/lib/affiliates.ts`, `src/server/affiliates.ts`, SQL `commerce.affiliate_*`): a signed-in customer gets a code and link at `/account/referrals`; the program needs the bonus program on. The referred friend (signed in, no earlier paid order in the store) gets `friendPercent` (10 %) off the goods of their first order, after campaigns and the group discount and before codes and credits: `orders.referral_discount_minor` is part of `discount_minor`, with each line's share, and `friendState()` is the only place it is decided, so `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()` agree (`checkout-kinds.int.test.ts`). The referrer earns bonus credits (`bonus_entries.kind = 'referral'`, `rewardBps` 5 %, for the friend's first `rewardOrders` paid orders, pending as the bonus program's `pendingDays`, a monthly cap per referrer) granted by a trigger when the order is paid and taken back by refunded share or on cancellation; the referral entries carry no order id, so the friend's own bonus maths is untouched. Guards in SQL: never oneself, never a friend who has ordered, never a blocked referrer, never copied or host orders; one `affiliate_attributions` row per order, append-only. Attribution: `?ref=` is kept in memory and on the cart, and in a cookie (`kaizen_ref`, `kaizen_aff_{storeId}`, marketing) only after consent. Visits are counted by day and code, nothing about the visitor. The AI manager reads and sets both programs (`get_/set_referral_program`, `get_my_referrals`, `get_/set_affiliate_program`, `block_affiliate`; changes are gated). Known limits: refunds made in Stripe's dashboard, renewals and guests do not count at the store level; a bare refund on a plan invoice without a credit note is not seen; the first subscription invoice is finalised at once and cannot take credit; without consent a reload loses the link. |
| D132 | **A plan comparison: every feature in a table, the plans as columns, a box for what each includes** (`src/lib/plan-features.ts`, `src/server/plan-features.ts`, `commerce.plan_features` and `plan_feature_grants`). Platform admins edit it at `/admin/platform/plans/features`: one form saves the whole matrix in a transaction (a feature's name, description, category and place, new features, removal, and a tick per active plan; a plan not shown keeps what it had). It starts with 50 features grouped by category (catalogue, checkout, design, international, AI, operations, Kaizen), in no plan yet, so the admin decides what each plan includes. Store owners see the same table read only under Compare plans on their Billing page, once any feature is ticked. It describes the plans; it does not switch features on or off, so a limit that must be enforced still needs its own check where the feature is used. |

An owner saves the store's own agreement now (environment, customer number and API details, the markets to ship to); secrets are encrypted and shown only as their last four characters, and nothing is sent to any carrier. The registry says what each will do and what it needs (the exact credential fields are confirmed against each carrier's API documentation when its connection is built), and `ShippingCarrierAdapter` is the one interface each connection will implement (check, rates, pickup points, booking with labels, tracking). Agreements are never copied to a duplicated store. |
| D134 | **Posten / Bring is connected** (`docs/shipping-carriers.md`, `src/lib/bring.ts`, `src/server/carriers/bring.ts`, `src/server/bring-shipping.ts`), on the store's own Mybring agreement: a connection check on the carrier's page, and from an order the services and prices for the parcel, a pickup point for service 5800, booking with a label, tracking, and the order marked as sent with the tracking number. The store saves a sender address with the agreement. In the store's test environment Bring books test shipments and the order is not marked as sent. The label is fetched from Bring when printed, never kept. Delivery options and prices at checkout are not part of it yet (they change what shoppers pay). A booked shipment keeps `carrier_id`, `consignment_number` and `label_url` on `shipments`, and a carrier's last check is on `shipping_carriers`. |
| D135 | **Delivery options at checkout** (`docs/shipping-carriers.md`, `src/lib/delivery-options.ts`, `src/server/delivery-options.ts`, `DeliveryChoice`): the shopper gives a postal code at checkout and chooses between the market's flat rate and the services of the store's carrier (Posten / Bring for Norway), with pickup points for service 5800. Each offer is kept as a `delivery_quotes` row priced as shown (carrier price + standard VAT + the store's percentage and amount, in the country's currency), and the chosen one (`carts.delivery_quote_id`, read by `chosenDelivery()`) is the basket's shipping rate in both `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()`, so they agree and no carrier is called while placing an order. The flat rate stays as the default and the fallback when Bring does not answer, so a store keeps it set up; a subscription ignores the choice. | D136 | **PostNord is connected** for pickup points, tracking and checkout services (`docs/shipping-carriers.md`, `src/lib/postnord.ts`, `src/server/carriers/postnord.ts`), on the store's own API key. PostNord has no price API, so a carrier's checkout services have two pricing modes (`CHECKOUT_PRICING`): `carrier` (Bring's Shipping Guide price plus VAT plus the store's markup) and `store` (PostNord: the store enters, per country and service, the price with VAT and a free-above value, in `shipping_carriers.checkout_prices`). Several carriers can be on in a country: their services are listed together, named for the carrier, and one that fails leaves the others. A window is a checkout quote (`delivery_quotes.window_*`, held until the carrier's `expiresAt`), priced as a `carrier`-priced service (VAT and markup on Porterbuddy's price; a `displayPrice` already has VAT) and listed by date; the order keeps `orders.delivery.window`. Booking asks for the window again and books it with the fresh token (the shopper's has expired by then), books nothing if it is gone, and never marks an order sent in the test environment. | D138 | **Helthjem is connected** for service points, checkout services, booking, labels and tracking (`docs/shipping-carriers.md`, `src/lib/helthjem.ts`, `src/server/carriers/helthjem.ts`, `src/server/helthjem-shipping.ts`), on the store's own client credentials (a token kept until it expires). Like PostNord it has no price API, so its two services (home delivery, service point) are `store`-priced, each with the weight it takes at most (`maxWeightGrams`, applied to the cart at quote time). What is booked is the store's own transport solution for each service; home delivery is checked for coverage of the full address before it is booked. Booking and labels of Porterbuddy and Helthjem share one admin component (`ChosenDeliveryBooking`) and the label route (`carrierLabel()`). |
| D139 | **Product recommendations** (`docs/recommendations.md`): upsells, cross-sells and complements picked for each shopper, shown in a product grid that recommends (page builder → content grid) on product pages, the All products page, articles and any page, and used by the chat agent through a `recommend_products` tool. Candidates come from the store's own data (the owner's pairings, co-purchase, vectors, shared categories and tags, searches, the page's words, sales); hard rules in SQL and code keep out anything not for sale, already bought (account, email or this device's carts; bookings excepted), in the cart or hidden; kinds are decided in code. The store's AI may only reorder the best candidates and its reasons are checked; the line under a product is a fixed sentence with a title the store holds, never the model's words. What the tab remembers (views, searches, clicks) is in session storage only (`kaizen_rec`, listed as necessary: to confirm per country); the cart, wishlist, account and device orders are read from the request's cookies. Per store: on or off, AI on or off, a monthly token cap (counted from `ai_usage`), an upsell ceiling, a share of tabs held on the plain ranking, and goes-with / never-with / hide rules. Impressions, clicks, add-to-cart and the revenue of the orders that follow are recorded per ranking so the AI's can be compared with the plain one. |
| D140 | **Recommendations, finished** (`docs/recommendations.md`): category and tag pages become page roles (`category`, `tag`; the *Category products* / *Tag products* components draw the term's listing, so a content grid that recommends can sit around it and recommends around the term); the report compares the AI's ranking with the plain one on tabs that clicked and tabs that added to cart with a two-proportion test, and says in plain words whether to keep the AI; a check replays past paid orders (one product held back, the order itself left out of what the engine learns from) against best sellers, free and without a model; and the AI manager can read and change the recommendations (`get_recommendations`, `set_recommendations`, `add_recommendation_rule`, `remove_recommendation_rule`, `check_recommendations`; the changes are kept for approval). |
| D141 | **Every store numbers its orders in one sequence, without gaps** (some countries require it). Each store has an `order` series from 1001 (`commerce.document_series`, made with the store); `placeOrder()` and the subscription renewal take the number inside the transaction that inserts the order, so an order that is rolled back gives its number back. The database now holds the rest: an order keeps its number and is never deleted while its store exists (a cancelled order stays, cancelled); the order, invoice and credit note series keep their prefix, move one at a time, and are not deleted once a document is issued in them; a store with no orders yet may still choose where its numbers start. Orders copied from another store (D129, `C-…`) are history outside the sequence. `commerce.order_number_audit(store)` checks a store's sequence (gaps, numbers not from the series, a series out of step), `orderNumberAudit()` reads it, and the AI manager's `store_checkup` reports a broken sequence. Production's four stores were checked when this shipped: all in order (the demo store 1001–1064). |

Chosen at `/admin/platform/pages` under *Special pages*, each with a *Start from a new page* that makes and publishes a starter (the front page's has the standard words, the buttons and the Plans component, D142). | D145 | **AI usage shows what it costs** (supersedes D106's "money is not shown"). Prices are data, never code: `commerce.ai_model_prices` holds US dollars per million input and output tokens for a provider's model, set at `/admin/platform/ai/prices` (Settings), a price never changing in place (a new price counts from now; a model's first from the beginning, so usage already recorded is priced). A call is priced by the price in force when it was made, matched on provider and model exactly or as the start of a dated version's name (`matchPrice()` in `src/lib/ai-cost.ts`, `priceFor` in `src/server/ai-usage.ts`); cost is worked out in millionths of a dollar in SQL and code, never by the model. Every page that shows tokens shows cost beside them: the platform's and an owner's usage reports (totals, per day, per provider and model, per owner account, store and feature), a store's recommendations cap ("used this month"), and the AI manager's usage answers (`estimated_cost_usd`). Models used without a price show "No price", the totals a "+", and the prices page lists them with a link to set one. The three models the platform already used were priced from their providers' lists when this was added and marked to be checked. |
| D146 | **Pictures, speech and live calls are priced too** (extends D145, which priced tokens only). `commerce.ai_model_prices` gains `per_image` (US dollars per picture), `per_audio_minute` (speech to text and live voice calls, by the call's seconds) and `per_million_characters` (text to speech), each null for "no price" and 0 for free; the price form leaves them empty when a model has none. A call's cost is its tokens plus its units at those prices, worked out once in `src/server/ai-price-sql.ts` (`priceFor`, `costMicrosSql`, `unpricedSql`, shared by the usage reports, the unpriced list and the recommendations' monthly figure) and in code by `callCost()` (`src/lib/ai-cost.ts`), which a test holds to the SQL. A call that made pictures, listened or spoke is unpriced when its model has no price or none for that unit, and shows "No price" with a "+" on the totals; a call that reported tokens is priced by them, so a model billed in tokens for its pictures or audio needs no unit price (and should have none, which would count twice). The prices page lists models unpriced for any of this. |

Website and Settings open on pages of cards (`/admin/{store}/website`, `/settings`), Settings' under headings. Sections are data (`src/lib/store-nav.ts`), tested against the folders of the store's admin and the admin map, whose `group` is now the page's section.
