Page wrappers of `max-w-2xl`…`5xl` are removed so pages use the width. A tab that is exact (a page of cards) still marks the pages listed as its own (`also`), and not those below its address. |
| D148 | **A/B testing for store owners and the platform is designed before it is built** (`docs/ab-testing.md`). Decided: only visitors who accepted the `statistics` category, and signed-in customers (subject to a legal check before the first live test), are enrolled, everyone else sees the original and is not counted; version 1 tests whole pages and parts of pages, never prices, discounts, shipping or legal pages; owners and staff can run tests (platform admins on Kaizen's pages); it is a paid-plan feature through one row in the plan comparison. Variants are served from the cache: enrolled visitors, found by a cookie, are rewritten to the variant's page by a `proxy.ts` (an experiment start or stop is a database change, no deploy), everyone else never reaches it. The phase 0 spike proved this locally (+0.5 to 0.9 ms) and found that a browser's cached copy goes stale after assignment (a page marker and one reload fix it) and that a dynamic hole flickers; the check on Vercel's network and the proxy's region are still open; one running test per target; variants locked once running; one goal per test, verdict only after the planned minimum, said in plain language with an estimate of the runtime before launch. Phase 1 is built (`docs/ab-testing.md`, "What phase 1 built"): the engine, tests of whole store pages, the admin at `/admin/{store}/experiments`, the proxy, results computed on read and the guardrail job; versions are pages of type `variant`, a page in a running test cannot be unpublished or have a change published, and the migration is `ab_experiments`. Phase 2 adds tests of a part of a page (a row, column or component chosen in the builder with "A/B test this"; a version may differ from the original in that part only, and applying a winner puts only the part into the page as it is then) and a scheduled start, the migration `ab_part_tests`. Phase 3 adds tests of a store's header, footer and product layouts: a visitor's versions travel in the market part of the address the proxy rewrites to (`no~{test}{version}`, taken off again by `resolveShop()`), so every page and the product pages are served from the same routes, and the migration is `ab_site_tests`. Phase 4 adds the AI manager's seven A/B test tools (list, explain, suggest, draft, start, stop, apply): the model words and drafts, code counts and decides, the three that change what visitors see are kept for the owner's yes and checked first; no migration. Phase 5 adds the platform's read-only view of every store's tests at `/admin/platform/experiments`, with the ones that need a look first (a version lowering orders, an uneven split, nobody seeing a running test, a test past its end or left undecided); no migration. Phase 6 emails the store's owners, and whoever made the test, once each when the guardrail stops a test, with the counted figures and what they may do; no migration. Phase 7 moves the search test (D77) and the recommendations held-out ranking (D140) onto the engine's arithmetic (`src/lib/experiment-units.ts`: the same comparison of rates, split check and rule for calling a difference, the old formulas kept in `experiment-legacy.ts` and checked to give the same numbers on the same data) and lists them beside page tests in the platform's view; they keep their units (a search, a tab), their own logs and no cookie, and make no experiment row, so no migration. Phase 8 gives the platform's AI manager two read-only tools over every store's tests (`list_ab_tests`, `explain_ab_test`), with a playbook; it has no tool that starts, stops or changes a store's test, since a platform admin cannot, and says what to tell the owner instead; no migration. Phase 9 lets a store test a modal (a popup that opens by itself may be left out of a version, to see whether it helps; every modal keeps its address name) and the rows around the shop's own component on its working pages (cart, checkout, order confirmation, My account, sign-in, wishlists, a subscription, weekly deliveries: by a part only, served from the page's own route like a header's, the shop's logic never varied); the migration is `ab_working_pages`. Phase 10 makes the store's front page and All products page testable, whole or by a part, served from their own routes like a working page and held in place while a test runs (`commerce.page_place()`, migration `ab_front_pages`); a part test compares the original as it would be saved, since saving keeps only the store's own languages. Phase 11 adds the goal "more people sending a form" (`form`, an email form or newsletter sign-up chosen on the page, counted once per visitor in their version when the site accepts the answer: `recordFormSent()` from `submitForm()`; migration `ab_form_goal`). |
| D149 | **The admin's look.** The admin was black and white: clean, but flat and hard to tell apart. Its colours are still the semantic ones every admin page is written in (`bg-background`, `bg-surface`, `border-border`, `text-muted`, `bg-foreground`), but `src/app/admin/admin.css`, imported only by the admin's root layout and scoped to `html[data-admin]`, now gives them a cool tint (a tinted page under white cards), one brand colour (indigo; `bg-foreground` buttons and badges, the current page, focus and selected states), soft shadows, and motion: fields ease to a brand ring on focus and a red border once touched and wrong, buttons lift and glow on hover and sink when pressed, links and rows warm up, a bar runs along the top while a page loads (`AdminProgress`), loading placeholders sweep with light, a form that is working shows a spinner on its button, and a page, a dialog, a saved message and an opened disclosure ease in. Light and dark follow the account's choice (D99); every motion stops with `prefers-reduced-motion`; nothing moves layout. The storefront, Kaizen's own site and the previews of a store's pages drawn in the admin (`data-theme-canvas`, `data-theme-preview`) are untouched, so a new admin page needs no styling of its own and never a fixed colour. |
| D150 | **The AI can copy another website's page into a store's page builder, and show its work.** On the store's AI studio page (`/admin/{store}/pages/ai`) the owner gives an address, a number of improving passes (1–10) and confirms they may copy the page; a job (`commerce.page_replications`, one active per store, migration `page_replications`) then runs step by step while the page is open (`tickReplication()`, one unit of work per request, a lock, resumable, stoppable with Abort): the page is opened in Chromium at computer and phone width (`replicate-open.ts`, `@sparticuz/chromium` on Vercel, the same launcher as the cookie scan), its boxes and styles read (`extractPage()`), the site's AI describes its design from photographs, the text is taken as it is, pictures, videos and Google fonts are downloaded as the store's own, and the page is built as a draft (`buildReplica()`) from what was *measured*: sections become rows, boxes side by side columns, pieces of content blocks, and every size, space and colour is the original's own, kept as a style model written into the page's CSS (D100). The model never writes structure. Each pass photographs the draft at both widths through a token-guarded preview page (`/admin/account/replica/{job}`), scores it against the original (`compareRasters()`, a quarter-size comparison in bands), sets spacing right by measuring (`calibrate()`), and lets the AI propose changes from pictures of the weakest places as a short list of declarations on parts that exist (`parsePatchPlan()`, `applyPatchPlan()`: allowed properties, clean values, at most 40 a pass); a copy that is as close as pixels allow ends early. The owner sees the steps, a bar, a log, the original beside the copy as it grows (scrolling together, with each pass's match), and at the end a summary written in code from the facts (what went well, what failed, the match at both widths) with links to preview and edit the draft. Safety: only public http(s) addresses (`parseReplicaUrl()`, `isBlockedAddress()`), every fetch through `safeFetch()` (every address of the name checked, the connection pinned to the checked address, redirects rechecked, size and time limits) and the browser's own requests refused the same way; the owner confirms the right to copy; the copy is a draft hidden from search engines. See [`page-replicator.md`](page-replicator.md). Limits: 50 rows, 100 blocks and 50 000 characters of CSS per page; forms, carousels, fonts outside Google Fonts and pages behind a sign-in are not copied. |
| D151 | **A picture keeps its own size.** In the page builder a picture block was stretched to its column; it is now drawn at the picture's stored width and height (for a crop, the largest crop of that shape inside it), never larger, and still shrinks to fit a narrower column or a phone, keeping its shape. Its Style tab has **Width** (pixels, `maxWidth`, down to 16, a slider and a number, with *Own size* to reset) and **Position** (left, centre or right by screen, `align`, the same choice as a button's; the caption follows). The block's frame (border, shadow, corners, spacing, id, classes and motion) is on a box as wide as the picture, not on the empty column beside it. | 2026-10-02 | Asked for by the owner: the picture's original height and width must be kept, with a way to make it smaller. Stored as two optional keys in the page's JSON (left out at the picture's own size and at the left), so there is no migration. The size is worked out only by `imageDisplaySize()`; `blockBox()` limits the box with the class `max-w-(--picture-width)` and a custom property, never an inline width, so owner CSS and the page replicator's rules can override it. Pixels were chosen over a percentage so the number means the same when the picture is replaced or cropped, and for a picture taken from a custom field, whose real size is not known (it stands in as 1600 × 1200, `BOUND_PICTURE_SIZE`). Consequences: existing pictures narrower than their column stop stretching and sit at the left (Position moves them); uploads are at most 1600 px, so a large one still fills a content column (the page is narrower than that), but in a wider, full-bleed row it now stops at its own size and sits at the left until Position moves it; a left or right margin set under Spacing wins over Position; copies made by the replicator keep their measured size because their own rules win. A *fill the column* choice is not offered. |
| D152 | **A store analytics cockpit with drill-down.** Under Analytics in the store admin: an Overview of twelve figures (revenue, net revenue, orders, conversion, average order, gross profit and margin, new and returning customers, refund rate, sessions, revenue per visitor) against the previous period and last year, with sales and profit charts, the funnel, best sellers, channels, a forecast, target progress, alerts and a plain-words explanation of why sales changed (traffic × conversion × basket, and the segment that moved most); then Finance, Customers (cohorts, RFM, LTV, repeat rate), Products, Inventory, Marketing (channels, CAC, ROAS, discounts, spend entry), Subscriptions, Traffic (devices, countries, searches, refunds, weekday and hour) and Settings. Every figure has one definition in `docs/analytics.md`, worked out in code. | 2026-10-02 | Asked for by the owner as the answer to four questions: are we making money, why are sales changing, which customers and products drive the business, what needs doing today. Costs are new data (`product_variants.cost_minor`, kept on the order line when sold) so profit is real, with payment fees, shipping costs and fixed costs as the owner's labelled estimates; profit always says how much of sales it covers and never shows zero for unknown. Visit counting is a new, off-by-default store setting without cookies, storage, IP address or user agent (a visitor hash that changes every store day, a whitelisted landing path, tied to a cart made that day for channel attribution), so conversion, funnel, devices and channels exist without a consent banner; its wording is deliberately not 'anonymous'. This supersedes D104's 'visits and product views are not tracked' while counting is on. Not tracked and said on the pages: refunds made only in Stripe's dashboard, return reasons beyond free text, subscription expansion and reactivation, regions inside a country, revenue after a search, anything before counting began. Reviewed adversarially by five lenses (25 defects confirmed and fixed, among them a blocker that stored secret link tokens in landing paths). |
| D153 | **The EU withdrawal function and a returns flow for goods.** A shopper withdraws from a contract at `/s/{store}/{market}/withdraw` (linked from every footer, order page and order email) in the two steps the directive asks for (*Withdraw from contract here*, then *Confirm withdrawal*), without giving a reason; the order number and email must match, the reply is the same whether they do or not, and nothing is a withdrawal until the second button is pressed (an unconfirmed request expires after 24 hours). Confirming records the statement, creates a return (`returns`, status `approved`) for the lines declared and emails the acknowledgement on a durable medium (the store, order, lines, date and time with offset, reference, what happens next), kept in `email_messages`; if the email fails the withdrawal still stands and staff see *acknowledgement not sent* with *Send again*. Per line, `lineEligibility()` (`src/lib/withdrawal.ts`) says whether the right applies and, if not, why in plain words: excluded by law, digital content with consent, business orders, a booking, already returned. The 14 days count only from a recorded receipt (`orders.delivered_at`, set by staff with *Mark received by the customer*): until then the right is open and no end day is shown. A store can offer a longer voluntary window (`return_settings.window_days`), whose part beyond 14 days is a *return request* it may decline. Staff work `/admin/{store}/returns` (queue with refund-due and overdue marks) and a detail page: approve or decline a voluntary return, instructions and return address, mark in transit and received, inspect per line (condition, restock, a deduction for diminished value that only inspection can make, with its note), then refund through the existing `refundOrder()` with the amount from `refundFor()` (what the shopper paid for the units after every discount kind, plus the original standard delivery only when the whole order is withdrawn, minus return shipping when the shopper pays it; a withdrawal's refund can be raised by staff but not lowered). The refund is claimed on the return before Stripe is called, so two staff cannot pay twice. Settings are the store's (`return_settings`, `/admin/{store}/settings/returns`, owners): window, transit estimate, who pays return shipping (kept with each order when it is placed, `orders.return_cost_payer`, and the standard delivery cost, `orders.standard_shipping_minor`), when the refund is made, excluded goods, instructions (translatable) and address. Staff can also register a withdrawal received outside the function. Returns appear in analytics (rate, reasons, products, time to refund, with minimum volumes), in the control center and store Home attention (overdue refunds, acknowledgements not sent), in the AI manager (`list_returns`, `explain_return`, and gated `approve_return`/`decline_return`) and in the store's structured return policy. | 2026-10-03 | Asked for by the owner as the first step to Shopify parity (`docs/shopify-parity.md`, gaps 1 and 11): the directive has applied since 19 June 2026 and `docs/plan.md` calls it a launch blocker. The legal act (the confirmed statement) is kept apart from the physical return, so a failing email or a slow refund never undoes a withdrawal. Matching without enumeration, limits counted in the database by hashed keys (never IP or cookie), and a withdrawal function that sets no cookie or storage keep it inside the privacy rules. A review by three adversaries (law, security and privacy, money) found 17 defects before shipping, all fixed with regression tests; the open points that need a lawyer (information given before the contract, services, sealed goods, receipt, deductions) are listed in `docs/returns.md` and every consumer text in Norwegian, Swedish, Danish and English needs human review. Not yet: return labels, exchanges, defect complaints, photos, ending subscriptions, and a withdrawal page the builder can edit. |
| D154 | **The parity tracker: Kaizen's gap to Shopify is data, checked by a test.** `docs/parity/rows/*.json` holds one row per Shopify feature (254 rows after removing 12 duplicates that the first report counted in two or three domains): the Shopify side (tier, what it does, the page it was read from and when), Kaizen's rating (full, partial, missing), a weight, the gap, a bucket (A build in-house, B needs a third party's approval, C needs a strategy decision, D not reachable by code), a wave, testable acceptance criteria and evidence (the files and tests that hold it, or `untested: true` as counted debt), and a history of every rating change. `src/lib/parity.ts` is the pure code (rules, scoring, per-wave projections, report renderers), `pnpm parity` prints the figures, `pnpm parity:write` regenerates the report's tables between the `parity:generated` markers, and `pnpm parity:check` and `src/lib/parity.test.ts` fail when a row breaks a rule (a Full row without existing files and a test or an `untested` flag, a rating change without a history entry, a fetched Shopify claim without a page and a date) or when `docs/shopify-parity.md` no longer equals what the data renders. `.claude/workflows/parity-wave.js` is the saved workflow every later wave starts from (`docs/parity/WAVES.md`). | 2026-10-03 | Asked for by the owner as wave 0 of the plan to Shopify parity (`docs/parity-plan.md`). The first report's headline was typed by hand and could not be re-checked; now it is computed, so a wave's claim is a row passing its criteria. The re-read moved the headline from 59.5% to 57.4% core (must-have 69.0%): 27 rating moves on core rows (D152 and D153 raised five, thirteen or more fell where the code did not hold the row's own criteria, such as search merchandising, segments, the workflow builder, store credit, guest-checkout settings and PCI scope), and the de-duplication. 35 rows that rested on memory were read from Shopify's pages; one still is not (it is listed). Weights are judgement and were not changed while ratings were. Nothing in this decision changes what stores or shoppers see. |
| D155 | **Custom grid items and a carousel with controls; the replicator makes grids and carousels from them.** A content grid (D51) has a fourth source, `custom`: up to 60 items the owner writes, each with a picture, title, text, link, button label, date, badge, a plain-text price and up to three detail lines (`CustomGridItem`, `src/lib/page-content.ts`, `src/lib/custom-grid.ts`). The price is only words: no `<Price>`, no VAT label, no cart, never a live price; a custom item never reaches structured data, the sitemap, llms.txt, search, feeds, recommendations, the knowledge base or the chat agent (`custom-grid-isolation.test.ts`; `blockText()` is empty for a custom grid). Links are the menu link kinds by slug (a copied page carries no foreign ids) or safe web addresses; a picture is an https file in this project's Storage or a site path (`custom-picture.ts`), checked in the schema, when drawn, when a template is applied and when items are copied. Categories, tags, filters and sort do not apply to this source. The editor has the item list (drag to order, duplicate, up and down), a *Copy current items* button that makes items from what a pages, articles or products grid shows now (prices left out, business-only or consumer-only products skipped, pictures that cannot be kept said), and notes on what custom items do not do. Item texts are translated and in the store worklist (detail lines by their own id, not their place), copies get fresh ids, a linked picture-only item is the link for keyboards. The carousel (shared with testimonials) takes `carousel` settings: arrows, dots, snap, rewind (the next arrow goes back to the first tile, real tiles only) and an opt-in autoplay that never runs under reduced motion, stops for good on any interaction, holds while the pointer is over it or the tab is hidden, and always has a Pause button; without JavaScript the row scrolls. The replicator (D150, `src/lib/replicate-grid.ts`, `replicate-watch.ts`) builds one grid block of custom items from repeated cards and from script sliders (Swiper, Slick, Splide and similar): cards must share a shape and size and every word and link of each card must fit an item, or the group stays as columns with the reason in the report; cloned slides are dropped, hidden slides read, a loop becomes rewind, arrows, dots and snap are read, autoplay is switched on only when two bounded captures saw the track move; a grid that scores under 60 % is tried as columns in the same pass and kept only if columns win by more than 2 points; no word in an item is one that was not in the capture (property-tested, and 0 invented words in 1,084 words on 26 real pages). No migration. Not built: a page-studio pattern using custom grids, a browser test of the builder's own editor (e2e has no admin sign-in; the editor's state changes are unit-tested). Known limits of the replicator, said in `docs/page-replicator.md`: cards inside one column of a side-by-side layout are not looked at (the largest gap: 24 of 25 sliders missed on one site), tablet columns are estimated (only 1440 and 390 px are captured), autoplay "not observed" means not seen in a few seconds, wrapped rows with a centred last row stay columns, and the grid-against-columns figures were measured before the last fixes. | 2026-10-03 | Asked for by the owner so the AI page replication can copy real grids and carousels. Plain-text prices keep a price from being shown that the checkout does not charge (Price Indication, Omnibus). Rewind instead of an infinite loop keeps screen readers from reading cloned tiles. Spec: `docs/content-grid-custom-items.md`; review by three lenses found 21 points, 18 fixed, one decided (custom pictures have no library alt fallback: empty alt means decorative) and two documented as not built. |
| D156 | **Wave 1 of the parity plan: compliance and money correctness.** `docs/wave-1.md` is the contract for the 18 rows of wave 1, in seven units run in two lanes: the money lane (1a tax profile and VAT engine, 1b invoices and credit notes, 1c VAT, OSS and IOSS reports, 1d unit price) and the trust lane (1e legal starters, terms at checkout, accessibility and PCI hardening, 1f staff two-step sign-in, roles and the activity log; 1g GDPR after 1b). Every rule that comes from a law carries its source and the date it was read and is flagged for an accountant or lawyer; every consumer legal text is hand-written in nb, sv, da and en. Defaults taken: destination VAT for consumers is kept, IOSS above 150 EUR is not handled (marking and reports only), TOTP without passkeys, 24 months of audit log. |
| D157 | **Tax profile and VAT engine (wave 1, unit 1a).** VAT categories are data (`commerce.vat_categories`: standard, exempt, accommodation, food, books, periodicals, medicines, culture_events, children_goods; products reference them by foreign key). Per-country rates have validity dates, a source, a checked-on date and a verified flag (`commerce.vat_rates`), are written only by `commerce.set_vat_rate()` (a period ends, a new one begins, audit-logged, never edited in place) and read only through `commerce.vat_rate(country, category, at)`; a missing row means the standard rate, and shipping takes the standard rate unless a person has verified a per-country rule. A store's tax profile (`commerce.store_tax_profile`: VAT, OSS, IOSS registration) is edited by owners at `/admin/{store}/settings/tax`; `duplicate_store()` copies the choices and blanks the numbers. `vatTreatment()` (pure) decides standard, reverse_charge or ioss with a reason code, through one loader (`tax-treatment.ts`) shared by `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()`. VIES is reached only through an injectable fetch with a fixed host, is never called while an order is placed, and its failure never blocks and never exempts. A reverse-charge order stores the VAT not charged inside `discount_minor` (`vat_relief_minor`), as D130 does for credits, so the total checks hold. IOSS marks private buyers' shipped goods of at most 150 EUR from outside the EU. Rows: the EU VAT row stays partial (report 1c, invoice 1b); the b2b row, `checkout.oss-and-ioss-support` and `international.ioss` become partial. Accepted limits: seeded rates are unverified and cover only NO, SE, FI and DE reduced rows; every consumer text is hand-written and needs legal review. Known limits: VIES says a number is registered, not whose it is, so a private buyer who types a company and someone else's valid number is not charged VAT (staff see a flag on the order); the VIES budget per store and hour can be used up by many carts. Migrations: `20261003193641_tax_engine`, `20261003193902_tax_engine_rules`. |
| D158 | **Legal starters, terms at checkout, accessibility checks, a strict checkout policy, and staff security (two-step sign-in, roles, activity log).** (1e) A store makes draft starter pages for terms, privacy, returns, shipping, withdrawal information, an imprint and an accessibility statement in nb, sv, da and en from its own facts (`legalFacts()`, `src/lib/legal-starters/*`), hand-written, each with a review notice and `[[placeholders]]`; they are published only by the owner and are linked roles (`LEGAL_ROLES`, in `page_roles`, never A/B tested). Checkout shows a terms and privacy link or a required checkbox (`stores.terms_at_checkout`); the pay press records `order_terms` with immutable `legal_snapshots`. The builder checks a draft (alt text, empty links, heading order, contrast, placeholders in every language) and asks before publishing with a blocking issue, recording the choice; CI scans the storefront and the admin's signed-out pages with axe. The cart, checkout and order routes send a Content-Security-Policy (`src/lib/csp.ts`, with `unsafe-inline` for scripts and Stripe's origins including maps.googleapis.com), draw no consent manager, owner code or chat widget (`OffPayRoutes`), are entered by full page loads, and refuse an HTML block on the checkout page. (1f) TOTP through Supabase Auth with our own single-use recovery codes, required of platform admins and, by the owner's switch, of a store's members; `getAccount()` fails closed and reads enrolment from the signed claim or a server-side `getUser()`. Roles are `store_roles` of `{section}:{read|write}` keys; every admin page, action and route asks a guard (scan tests against a baseline); `staff:write`, `billing:write` and owner-only pages stay the owner's; collaborators are members with a role and an expiry. `audit_log` gains area, target and allowlisted changes, has a filtered page and an owner's CSV, and is pruned at 24 months. *Why:* Asked for by the owner (wave 1). Texts need legal review, the accessibility audit and SAQ A are the company's, passkeys and native recovery codes wait. Rating: legal, accessibility and PCI partial (bucket D), two-step partial until the platform admin's enrolment in production is recorded; roles and audit log Full. The two roles rows are one row after the merge (`platform.staff-accounts-and-roles`, Partial until a real two-step enrolment is recorded). Migrations: `legal_terms`, `legal_terms_rules`, `staff_security`, `staff_security_rules`, `store_role_templates`, `plan_features_wave1`. |
| D159 | **Invoices and credit notes for shop orders in the store's own gap-free series.** A paid order is invoiced by `commerce.issue_order_invoice()` called inside `complete_order_payment()` in a sub-block (every paid path: checkout, renewal, standing delivery, venue booking; a failure rolls back only itself, so the number returns and the payment stands). A succeeded refund, by any path, gets a credit note from a deferred constraint trigger at commit (`refundOrder()`, the new Stripe `refund.created`/`refund.updated` handler `applyStripeRefund()`, D153 returns, a refund made in Stripe, raw SQL), and a return refunded outside Kaizen gets one from the return. The credit note is allocated by `creditAllocation()` (largest remainder over the invoice's VAT-rate buckets, or the return's own working in `returns.refund_working`) and is never above what the invoice has left per rate (a database trigger); the VAT in the seller's and the main currency is a difference of cumulative conversions, so notes sum to the invoice's. A refund of a payment that is not on the invoice (a no-show fee) gets none (`payment_on_invoice()`, order event `credit_note.not_invoiced`). Documents are frozen JSON snapshots, immutable except a one-time `pdf_path` and `anonymise_expired_documents()` (unit 1g's hook, 5-year floor), with no delete and no direct insert. Copied, host, Stripe-test-mode (`payments.test_mode`, frozen at payment for venue payments) and invoicing-off orders get none; a store with incomplete seller details, a non-VAT-registered seller whose order charged VAT, or no exchange rate waits and is issued by the five-minute job with the payment day as supply date. Invoicing is on for new stores and off for stores with live history until the owner turns it on (no backdating). Content follows Art. 226 and the national lists of NO, SE, DK and DE (sources and gaps in `docs/wave-1b-invoices.md` 1.3), with the VAT in the seller's currency (Art. 230), reverse charge with both numbers and the IOSS number from D157; a non-registered seller's document carries no VAT columns. The PDF is the same view rendered by Chromium (`src/server/invoice-pdf.ts`, every request refused), stored once in the private `documents` bucket, the print page the fallback; documents are served by long random tokens at `/s/{store}/{market}/account/documents/{token}` (no cookie, noindex, outside the tracking extras), linked in the confirmation, shipped and refund emails with the PDF attached (base64) when it exists. The Stripe invoice option is ignored while Kaizen invoicing is on. New: refund webhook events, `returns.refund_working`, a base64 email attachment, `refundKey()` independent of webhook-written refund rows. All consumer wording is hand-written nb, sv, da, en and needs legal review. Rows: `orders.invoices-and-vat-receipts-for-orders`, `orders.credit-notes-for-refunds`, `international.legal-invoices-and-credit-notes-for-orders`, held at Partial until the wording is reviewed and a real PDF is read per country. *Why:* the largest weighted gap after returns; draft orders, order editing, gift cards, payouts and unit 1c all need correct documents. Migrations: `order_invoices`, `order_invoices_rules`, `order_invoices_audit`, `order_invoices_fixes`, applied to production on 2026-10-04 in parts (versions in the table below); the old append-only triggers were replaced with `CREATE OR REPLACE TRIGGER`, so the tool's cancelled DROPs were not needed; the two redundant indexes `credit_notes_refund_idx` and `invoices_order_idx` are left for the owner to drop. |
| D160 | **Unit price indication: the price per kilogram, litre or metre beside the price.** A goods variant carries a total measure (g, kg, ml, cl, l, cm, m, m2, piece) and the owner's preferred comparison base (`product_variants.measure_amount/measure_unit/measure_base`). The shop shows the price per measure on the product page (following the chosen variant and purchase option), listing cards (for the variant the card's price shows), the cart and slide-out cart, checkout, the order page, the order emails, the chat's product cards, and the subscription and weekly-delivery lists. The product's JSON-LD carries an `Offer.priceSpecification` (`UnitPriceSpecification` with `referenceQuantity` and `valueReference`); `unitPricingMeasure` is a Merchant Center feed name, not schema.org, and waits for the feed in wave 5. The figure is only ever worked out by `unitPrice()` (`src/lib/unit-price.ts`: integers, half up to the minor unit) from the price as shown. A euro view or a business buyer's net price therefore gets its own figure, never a converted one (435 cents for 250 g is 1740 per kg, where converting the krone figure would give 1739). It is omitted when it equals the price or the price is 0, and it never changes a total: it is display plus a frozen snapshot on the order line (`order_lines.measure_*`, copied by `placeOrder()`, the subscription renewal and `copy_orders()`). A product marked `sold_by_measure`, or in a product category marked `requires_unit_price` (or under a marked ancestor), cannot be saved active while an active physical variant has no measure (`commerce.unit_price_required()` plus a deferred constraint trigger); existing products are grandfathered and reported (`productsNeedingMeasure()`). A measure on a service or download is refused. Basket-level discounts, codes, campaigns, credits and reverse-charge relief do not change a line's unit price; a reduced list price does; the 30-day reference never gets one. A market's country decides the base (`UNIT_PRICE_COUNTRY_RULES`): no country allows 100 g or 100 ml today (Germany, Norway and Sweden read, Denmark unread and closed), so every market compares per kg, l, m, m2 or piece. | 2026-10-04 | Asked for by the owner (wave 1, docs/wave-1.md 1d): the Price Indication Directive 98/6/EC and its national forms; Shopify has it natively. Not built: a unit price on invoices, sorting by unit price, the Merchant Center feed attributes, loose goods, dosage and drained-weight units. The row `international.unit-price-indication` stays Partial until a person has read the country table, the discount rule (Art. 6a's prior unit price was not read), the Norwegian and Danish rules and the nb/sv/da/en words (`docs/wave-1d-unit-price.md` sections 4 and 8). Orders with no document are counted and itemised by cause in a reconciliation that ties the report to Finance and to sum(`tax_minor`) of paid orders in every currency. Books mode counts a credit note in its own period; filing mode follows the Commission's OSS guidelines (a later-period credit is a Part 3 correction of the sale's period). Seller country, member state of identification and dispatch country are frozen on the order's `vat_treatment`. Four fixed, formula-safe CSV layouts, an export log with a drift line, deadlines, registration warnings and read-only AI tools. | D162 | **GDPR export, erasure and a retention schedule (wave 1, unit 1g).** A register (`PERSONAL_DATA`, `src/lib/personal-data.ts`) names every table that can hold a person's data, how it links to them, what the export says and what erasure does; three detectors (by column name including `*_name`, by foreign key to a shopper anchor, by free-text or payload column) fail `src/db/privacy.test.ts` for any table not in it, so a later wave cannot add personal data unnoticed. `exportCustomerData()` gives one JSON file (profile, addresses, orders with lines, payments, refunds with their reasons, shipments, invoices and credit notes, returns and withdrawals, subscriptions and standing lists, wishlists, bonus, referrals, consents held under the person, emails, carts, custom fields) to staff (`customers:write`, a POST) and to the shopper after a fresh sign-in (10 minutes, five downloads an hour); the file is never emailed and never holds another store's or another person's data. A shopper's export and erasure act only on the account's own proven email, never on an address typed on an order. `eraseSubject()` closes the account and removes or restricts every register entry: orders under the bookkeeping duty are restricted (`orders.restricted_at`, cut loose from the person, unused, never emailed, relinked or copied) and anonymised by `commerce.anonymise_order()` through the daily retention run after the seller's country's period (NO 5, SE 7, DK 5, DE 8, other 10 years from the end of the calendar year of the sale), unpaid and copied orders are cancelled and anonymised at once after the Stripe session is closed, subscriptions are cancelled, email bodies are blanked (rows kept for their keys), the email opt-out is kept; nothing deletes an order (D141) and invoices change only through `anonymise_expired_documents()` (D159). `commerce.retention_rules` holds every period with source, basis, date and a reviewed flag (written only by `set_retention_rule()`, five-year floor for bookkeeping); `runRetention()` runs daily from the subscription-reminders cron in application code with a clock argument and ends the gaps found in this read (customer codes and sessions, email bodies, carts, quotes, webhook payloads, orders). `commerce.privacy_requests` carries the one-month clock of GDPR Art. 12(3), extension to three months and a reasoned refusal. Every export and erasure is audit-logged with counts and ids only. All consumer wording is `src/lib/privacy-text.ts`: hand-written nb, sv, da, en, needing legal review. Retention periods are unverified by an accountant. Row rated Partial: no route test or e2e for the staff paths yet. *Why:* the row `international.gdpr-data-export-and-erasure`; the account delete left names, addresses and emails in orders, email logs and webhook payloads for ever, and a running subscription billing nobody. Migration versions: `20261004174355_gdpr`, `20261004174402_gdpr_rules`, `20261004174410_gdpr_audit`, recorded in the table under Migration versions. |
| D163 | **A model that sees pictures, chosen and checked in the AI settings.** Pictures go to the AI in two places (copying another site's page, `src/server/replicate.ts`, and writing alt texts, `src/server/alt-texts.ts`), and until now both used the one text model, which may not see; the owner had no way to know, and a copy then "corrected by measuring only". `ai_providers.vision_model` (same provider and key as the text model; empty = the text model) is a field of its own in the form, `/admin/platform/ai` and `/admin/{store}/settings/ai`: the provider's models that usually see pictures are one press each (`visionModels` in `src/lib/ai-provider.ts`, with what each is good for), any name can be typed, and *Check that it sees pictures* sends the model a small picture of two coloured bands (random colours, `src/lib/ai-vision.ts`, `src/server/ai-vision.ts`) and asks which colours they are, so a text-only model that refuses, or only guesses, is found out (one in twelve guesses right) before anything relies on it. Everything that sends a picture goes through `seeing(connection)`, which swaps the vision model in as the text model; the connection itself keeps the quick text model for searches. Both settings pages and the page copier's panel say plainly whether a model that sees pictures is set up and link to the field (`#vision`). The list of suggested models is a start, never a claim: the check is the authority. | Decided |

It is a trial: the rows stay only if the page, measured again, matches no worse (`BACKDROP_WORSE`), and they go back if the look at the rebuilt page fails; a failed look is tried again once. A group of more than six cards is never tried as columns, and a row's strips are kept together (two library files a row). | D168 | **Redirects, a 404 report and SEO fields for categories and tags (wave 2, second run).** `docs/wave-2-redirects.md` is the contract. A changed handle of a product that was live, and a changed slug of a category or tag, leave an automatic permanent redirect (`commerce.redirects`, kinds `product`, `category`, `tag`, made by database triggers, pointing at the thing so they never chain; a thing taking an address replaces its automatic redirect); pages and articles keep `page_redirects`. | D171 | **A store can be closed, and suspended and reopened by the platform; nothing is deleted.** `docs/store-closure.md` is the contract. An owner closes from *Settings, Close store* after a sign-in from the last ten minutes and the store's address typed in; it is blocked while paid goods are unsent or subscriptions and weekly deliveries run, and warns about the rest. Closing ends the Kaizen plan with its period (first: if Stripe refuses, nothing is closed), cancels orders waiting for payment, releases the store's own domains and emails the owners. The owner may reopen for 30 days; the platform suspends, closes (forcibly if need be) and reopens at any time, with a reason the owners are emailed. The database holds the steps (`stores_status_rules()`) and refuses a new order in a store that is not open (`orders_store_open()`); `memberCan()` limits every role in such a store to orders, customers and analytics and the owner's own key; jobs that email shoppers or call a model skip it (`store_is_active()`). **Permanent deletion of a store that never sold was not built:** orders, invoices and the audit log cannot be deleted and almost no foreign key cascades, so a purge would be a large, risky ordered delete for little gain. | D172 | **Inventory with locations, an adjustment history, backorders and low-stock notices (wave 3, first run).** `docs/wave-3-inventory.md` is the contract. Every change of `inventory_levels.on_hand` is a row of the append-only `commerce.inventory_movements`, written by a trigger so every writer is covered (reason, source, staff account, order, return; 24 months, except each level's newest movement, kept as the baseline). `inventory_ledger_check()` proves the movements add up to the level. A variant can keep selling at zero (`stock_policy = 'continue'`, physical goods only, `backorder_days` 1 to 90 required, stated on the product page, cart, checkout, order page and confirmation, frozen on the order line). Only such a variant's level may go below zero, enforced by a trigger. A checkout's claim on the stock it was told was there is kept whatever the order of payment (`inventory_reservations.backorder_quantity` marks the part beyond stock; `commerce.draw_order_stock()` draws it inside `complete_order_payment()`). Stock is read through one view, `commerce.variant_availability` (`in_stock` floors each location at zero). Locations have a rank (`priority`); `placeOrder()` allocates with `allocate()` under row locks taken in (variant, location) order; refund, cancel and return restock to the original location or one staff choose (`restockPlan()`, `restockRoom()`). Staff add, rename and rank locations; owners deactivate after a report of the units that stop being for sale. An Inventory page (on hand, committed to checkouts in progress, available, owed; adjust with a reason; history), stock files as D165 jobs (`inventory_import`, `inventory_export`, dry run, `on_hand_was` conflicts), and a per-variant low-stock level sending the owners one digest email per crossing (`stock_alerts`). | D173 | **Order search, filters, saved views and bulk actions; tags and archive; draft orders with pay links; gift messages (wave 3, run 2).** `docs/wave-3-orders.md` is the contract. The Orders page reads its state from the address (`parseOrderListParams()`: search over number, email, name, line title and SKU, tag and tracking number; filters for status, payment, fulfilment, tag, market, source, gift, archive and a store-day range; keyset paging, 50 a page, a count capped at 10,000; the total sort compares totals converted at the store's rates). Every list condition is built by `orderListWhere()`. Saved views are the store's (30 at most). Bulk actions (tag, untag, archive, unarchive, mark sent, print slips; 250 at most, slips 100) run order by order in their own transactions and report each refusal. Orders have tags (`order_tags`: 40 characters, 250 per order, case-insensitive) and an archive state (`orders.archived_at`, a visibility flag only; the number sequence is untouched; a return unarchives; automatic archiving is an owner option); both work on copied orders (two guard functions patched). Draft orders (`draft_orders`, `draft_order_lines`): staff price goods and custom service items with a custom price, a staff discount, shipping and a market through `priceDraft()`, built from the checkout's own VAT, shipping and allocation functions and equal to `cartSummary()` for the same goods. Send makes the order (`source = 'draft'`) from the one shared insert `src/server/order-insert.ts`, takes its number from D141's sequence and holds stock until the draft's expiry; the buyer pays through a hashed-token link (`/account/pay/{token}`, Stripe's hosted page, one idempotency key per press), or staff record money taken outside Kaizen (a `payments` row with provider `manual`, a method and a received day of at most 31 days back, owner-only unless the owner allows staff, cash ceilings held as unverified data in `cash-limits.ts`), which the invoice states as paid outside and whose refund is recorded with no Stripe call, capped under the order lock and by the `refunds_manual_cap` trigger, and gets a credit note by the existing trigger. An expired draft cancels its order, releases the stock and leaves no gap. A custom item is sold to businesses only until the legal review decides; a delivery address must be in the market's country. A staff discount is its own kind on the invoice. Gift messages (store switch, default off): `is_gift`, To, From and a 300-character message on the cart and the order, cleaned by `cleanShopperText()`, refused not cut, frozen after placement, erased with the order, printed on a price-free packing slip, never in a shipping notice and never sent to a third party. Rows: tags and archive, gift messages Full; order list and draft orders Partial (draft orders bucket D until a person has read the consumer texts of section 8). After the deploy: `commerce.order_number_audit()` is ok for all five stores; the security advisors show only the existing notices (none for the new tables or functions), the performance advisors INFO only. |
| D174 | **Orders edited after purchase, sent in parts, closed units, packing slips per parcel and pick lists (wave 3, run 3).** `docs/wave-3-fulfilment.md` is the contract. Every new shipment names its lines and units (`shipment_lines`, written only by `markSent()`; no lines given = everything still to send; shipments from before are `legacy` and count as everything sent). Units still to send are only `commerce.line_to_send()` / `toSend()`: quantity − shipped − withdrawn (withdrawal returns only, `withdrawn_quantity()`) − closed. An order is *Partly sent* and still `paid` while units are left, and `fulfilled` through `refresh_fulfilment()` when none are; `markDelivered()` needs it sent in full, so the 14-day receipt (D153) counts from the last parcel. Each parcel has its own carrier, tracking and email listing its lines; the shopper's order pages list the parcels and what is still to come. Units that will not be sent are closed explicitly (`commerce.unsent_closures`, append-only, under the order lock, never more than is to send): the admin refund's *These units were not sent* (`refundOrder({ notSent })`) takes restocked units off what is to send, a closed unit is never in a parcel, a slip or the pick list, and is never offered for withdrawal (`loadFacts()` counts it taken); a plain restock is a sent unit that came back. Editing (`order_edits`, E1, E2 … per order; `src/server/order-edits.ts`): only a paid order with nothing sent, standard VAT, no subscription, booking, box, host, return, venue balance or closed unit, in an open store (`editBlock()`). Kept units keep their sold price and discounts (`splitLine()`); added goods are priced by `priceOrderEdit()` with `shown()` and `decideTax()` and equal `cartSummary()`. Lines and amounts of a settled order change only inside the edit context (`kaizen.order_edit`; triggers refuse anything else). A lower total is claimed under the order lock and refunded through `refundOrder({ within: tx })` in the same transaction; a higher total applies only when the customer pays the link (`/account/change/{token}`, Stripe's hosted page; `applySession()` branches on `payments.order_edit_id`) or when staff record it paid outside Kaizen under D173's cash ceiling. `refundOrder()` now splits any refund over the order's captured payments (`splitRefund()`). A late change payment is refunded in full and moves no bonus or affiliate credit (`payment_of_order()`); a change refund that fails later is shown as owed (`order.edit_refund_failed`, checkup `edit_refund_failed`). The order number never changes (D141). Documents: an invoiced order's change issues an edit credit note and an additional invoice (`kind = 'order_edit'`), both referring to the original (`issue_edit_documents()`); later credits are pooled over the order's documents; a change's refund gets no credit note and is not a refund in analytics. Slips print what is still to send (`packingSlipData()`, up to 100 orders, each in its language, no prices) or one parcel (`parcelSlipData()`); the pick list (`pickList()`, `/admin/{store}/orders/pick-list`) sums the selected orders' units to send by product, variant and SKU or lists them by order. Tracker: `checkout.order-editing-after-purchase` was merged into `orders.edit-an-order-after-placement` (README rule 8). Packing slips and pick lists are Full; partial fulfilment and order editing stay partial until the consumer texts (change emails, pay page, parcel and receipt words, edit documents) are reviewed by a person, a change is paid in Stripe test mode and the staff screens are checked by hand; a carrier-booked partial parcel has no test yet. Migration versions (applied by CI on 7 October 2026, 07:00 UTC, after a first release stopped at `invoices_order_idx`, which production still had; the file now creates it only if missing): 20261006235329_fulfilment, 20261006235336_fulfilment_rules, 20261007022211_fulfilment_plan_features, 20261007041952_unsent_closures, 20261007042008_fulfilment_unsent_closures. After the deploy: `order_number_audit()` ok for all five stores; security advisors unchanged; performance advisors INFO only (one new unindexed key, `order_edit_lines_edit_fk`, indexed by the follow-up that also adds the shipment-lines rule). Follow-up applied by CI on 7 October 2026, 08:11 UTC: 20261007070937_shipment_lines_required (index `order_edit_lines_edit_idx`), 20261007070948_shipment_lines_required_rule (`shipments_have_lines`, a parcel that is not legacy has lines, checked at commit); the order-number audit still ok. A starter is not a real store: every reader of `is_template` that means so leaves starters out (`not (s.is_template or s.starter)`, held by `store-starters.scan.test.ts`), `store_is_active()` is false for it so jobs skip it, `orders_store_open()` refuses its orders (`orders.store_starter`), `sendEmail()` holds back its shopper and sign-in emails, the storefront is `noindex`, out of sitemaps and llms.txt, with a notice and the cart's reason instead of the checkout. Hidden starters (frozen copies, workspaces, deleted templates' stores) are worked in by nobody: `loadMembership()` honours a membership of a starter only while a `store_starters` row describes it, and Work's and Kaizen Life's store lists and `snapshotSources()` leave them out; `applyDesignPreset()` refuses them. | 2026-10-07 | Asked for by the platform owner: create a profile from a store or from scratch and configure the whole design from the profile's page, never by going to a store; draft, publish, unpublish, archive and delete for both. The place is now the pointer's among the columns (`columnSlotAt()` in `src/lib/column-slot.ts`: the line the pointer is in, the nearest where it is between lines, and the columns whose middle is before it, counting from above where the row stacks), for the Column tile, a saved column and a column moved by its handle (which counts without itself in its own line), shown by the indicator on the column it goes before. | 2026-10-10 | Asked for by the owner. No migration. Checked in a real browser: the gaps of a row of one line and of a row of two lines, before the first and after the last, and a moved column. |
| D190 | **Row width in the theme's Row.** The Theme tab's Row section has *Row width* (the content's width or the whole screen's) and *Content width* (what a full-width row holds), as a row's own settings do; every row takes them unless it chooses its own, which wins (also the content's width over a theme that spans the screen). `ThemeElement.width` and `contentWidth` are laid under rows by `layerUnder()`; a row's fields show what the row comes to and write its own only where that is not what it would be without it (`rowWidthPatch()`). | 2026-10-10 | Asked for by the owner. No migration (theme settings are JSON; a row stores `content` as well as `full`). `e2e/theme-row-width.spec.ts` holds the site. |

Each change goes into the page at once; Escape puts the words back, pressing elsewhere ends it. A double press and the wrench still open the settings (and end the in-place editing); blocks bound to a custom field and the translating view keep to the settings. | 2026-10-10 | Asked for by the owner. No migration. Checked in a real browser: typing, Enter, Escape, bold, the bar and pressing elsewhere. |
| D192 | **Staff edit a page's words on the live site.** Signed-in staff who may change the website get *Edit text* beside *Edit page* on a page or an article: a heading or a text of the page's own is pressed and typed in where it stands, then saved (Save, Ctrl+Enter, or Enter in a heading) or cancelled, without opening the page builder. The words are read and saved by `/api/platform/editor/text` against the revision that was read, into the published page and into the draft where it still has the same words (otherwise the builder's draft keeps its own and the person is told); the visitor's page only gains markers, and the editor is fetched when the mode is turned on. Not offered for parts shared with other pages or bound to a field, in a modal, on a working page, in a translation, for a page in a running A/B test or one that is not published; headers, footers and product layouts stay in the builder; on a store's own domain the admin's session cannot be seen, which D193 answers. | 2026-10-10 | Asked for by the owner. No test signs in to the admin, so the signed-in path through the real route is checked by hand. |
| D193 | **Editing words on a store's own domain.** Stores live on a domain of their own (P7), so the admin's sign-in cannot be seen there and *Edit text* (D192) would never appear to the staff who need it. For staff who came from the admin, *Edit text* on such a page is now a short visit to the admin: the admin checks, as *Edit page* does, that the person may change the store's website and sends the browser back to the store's host with a signed link token (ninety seconds, store and account, good for the exchange only); the store's host swaps it for a pass, an HttpOnly cookie of that host for half an hour that the editing routes (and nothing else) accept, and the page opens in editing. The pass is looked up again at every use, so removing a member, ending a collaborator's time or closing the store ends it at once; *Done editing* ends it on the spot. It is a deliberate, narrow exception to P7: a script on a store's page cannot read the pass, but while one is valid a script on that page can ask the editing routes to change the words of headings and texts, which is all the pass opens (nothing of the admin, no code, no other content). The second step is checked when the pass is given and not again for the half hour. | 2026-10-10 | Needed by D192's wording ("as long as I am logged in") on the stores the owner actually uses. No migration; the key is the existing `SETTINGS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. `edit-grant.test.ts` holds the tokens, `edit-pass.int.test.ts` the lookup at each use and `page-text-edit.int.test.ts` the routes' rules with a pass; `e2e/inline-edit-live.spec.ts` the refusals; the button, the visit to the admin, the return and *Done editing* were driven in a real browser against stand-ins for the admin and the routes. Not yet tried on a real store domain with a real sign-in. |

Buttons choose how they answer the pointer from thirteen effects (Fade, as before, Darken, Lighten, Lift, Grow, Shadow, Fill sweep, Invert, Underline, Arrow, Shine, Glow, None), per button or dual button, with the theme's default for every button of the site. | D207 | **Menu links answer the pointer with a line or a background, and show the page the visitor is on.** A menu component's links had only a light grey shade on hover. The link of the page shown carries the same effect unless switched off; it is marked in the browser (`aria-current="page"`), so pages stay cached and the same for all. A gliding marker needs script; without it each link grows its own. Times and easing follow the hover timing (D206). |
| D208 | **An FAQ's answers hold components.** Under each answer's text an owner can add up to twelve components of their own (text, heading, image, button, two buttons, separator, icon list, table, video, social links), added, ordered, edited and taken out from the question in the FAQ's General tab, and opened in their own settings by pressing them on the canvas. They are ordinary components inside the FAQ: their own ids, styles, typography, spacing and translations, copied with the FAQ (also as a template or a global part), checked for accessibility, and their words added to the FAQPage data for search engines. They never move, are never global or bound to a field, and show to every visitor, because an answer is what the FAQPage data says it is. A question shows once it has a text or a component; one answered by components without words (a button alone) is left out of the FAQPage data. Accordions and tabs are unchanged. |

It is a grid with its columns set per screen size (three, at most three on Medium and two on Small unless set), or the site's own carousel (D155) with as many in view; tiles take one shape (square, 4:3, 3:2, 16:9, upright 3:4, or each its own). A press on a tile opens it full size in a lightbox, the browser's own modal dialog, with the previous and the next by buttons, the arrow keys or a swipe, going round; without a script a tile is a link to the piece itself. A YouTube or Vimeo video still asks for its play press there, so nothing is fetched from them before a visitor chooses. With the lightbox off, pictures show as they are and videos play in their tiles. A picture with no alt text, or a video with no title, is the accessibility check's missing alt text. |

The fade follows the carousel's own settings: arrows, dots, a swipe across the tile on a phone (which opens nothing, and a swipe up or down still scrolls), going back to the first, and autoplay with its rules (never under reduced motion, stops at any interaction, waits under the pointer or with the tab hidden, a Pause button); the tiles not shown cannot be reached, and without a script the tiles are a row to scroll. The footer is pinned only after a scroll near the page's end; a page's or header's first row loads its pictures at once and first, the first grid its first four tiles; a background picture gives a phone the library's 480 px copy (only that when blurred); no visitor script imports zod or the page model (schemas in `*-schema.ts`, held by `storefront-bundle.scan.test.ts`); parts that only some pages draw are `next/dynamic` stand-ins in `components/lazy/` (never a form, whose typed words would be lost, or a booking's changes); the chat and referral capture wait until the browser is idle; font stylesheets are kept a year under a versioned address; an embedded frame is always named. `pnpm perf` runs Lighthouse; `e2e/performance.spec.ts` holds the first scripts' budget and the eager pictures. |
| D212 | **A visitor's browser gets only the interface text its client components read (`docs/performance.md`).** The four hand-written languages were one 270 KB module that every visitor's browser loaded. Each is now `src/lib/messages/{lang}.ts` (all read by `t()` on the server and in the admin), spreading in `{lang}-browser.ts` (the texts visitors' client components read through `browserText()`, about 4 KB) and `{lang}-returns.ts` (the withdrawal function's, `returnsText()`, loaded only on its page). A first version loaded only the page's language as a script of its own and made the page wait for it before waking up; presses in that moment were lost (a gallery's arrows in CI), so the small texts load with the page instead. Held by `storefront-bundle.scan.test.ts`; the demo front page's scripts went from 266 KB to 206 KB compressed. |
| D213 | **Pictures and videos compressed in the browser until they are as small as they can be while they look the same (`docs/media-compression.md`).** Pictures were written at a fixed 85 % and videos uploaded as they were. Now `compressPicture()` searches the lowest quality whose result passes `looksTheSame()` (SSIM on brightness and colour, measured as seen at half size with a full-size floor; the bar set on photos, a screenshot and a graphic and checked by eye), WebP or, for page pictures, AVIF from a WebAssembly encoder when a tenth smaller; `compressVideo()` re-encodes to at most 1080p H.264 with the browser's WebCodecs (mediabunny), dropping a background's sound, kept only at 80 % or less. Uploads say what they saved. `media.compressed_at` marks a compressed file; the media library's Compress makes older files smaller one or all at a time, uploads the result beside the original (addresses are cached for a year), moves every editable use to it in one transaction and deletes the original unless the file left the site or a document names it. No new service; nothing leaves the browser but the result. Migration version (applied by CI, 13 October 2026 09:41 UTC): `20261013075937_media_compressed`. Advisors checked after: no new warning; INFO only (RLS on, no policy, as every private table; unused indexes). |

| D237 | **Shipping methods, zones and profiles, local pickup and local delivery (wave 3).** `docs/wave-3-shipping-methods.md` is the contract. **Methods, zones and profiles.** Per market, named methods (`shipping_methods`) carry rates per zone and profile (`shipping_method_rates`). A rate is flat, by basket weight or by basket value. Tiers run from an inclusive minimum to an exclusive maximum with no gap, held by the deferred trigger `shipping_rates_tiers`. Zones are postal codes and prefixes inside a country (`shipping_zones`); the most specific pattern wins and Rest of country takes the rest. Profiles group products (`shipping_profiles`), with General for the rest. A mixed basket is offered a method only when every profile has a matching tier, and pays their sum, free above the method's value. Goods without a weight weigh the store's default per unit (`shipping_settings`, 500 g). **Flat rate.** The market's flat rate stays as the fallback when no method prices a basket. It is the only shipping of subscriptions, weekly boxes (`placeOrder({ flatRateOnly })`) and drafts. **Pickup and local delivery.** Pickup is set per place (`pickup_options`: price, ready time, instructions). Local delivery is set per area (`local_delivery_areas`), by postal codes or by a radius from a place measured between GeoNames postal-code centres (`postal_points`, CC BY 4.0, NO/SE/DK/FI). An area has a price, a minimum and a free-above value, and the cheapest matching area is offered. **Cart and order.** The cart holds one choice and the postal code. `chosenDelivery()` stays the one reader for `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()` and re-prices the store's own methods on every read. Orders keep `delivery_method`, a snapshot in `delivery`, the place and the driver's note. **Handovers.** *Ready for pickup* emails once (`order-ready:{id}`). *Collected* records a handover (`shipments.method = 'pickup'`) and `delivered_at` together; *Out for delivery* records a `local` handover. Neither kind of order is in *To send*. **Refunds.** The standard delivery kept for refunds is the cheapest option actually offered (`standardShippingMinor(offer)`). **Postal-code mismatch.** A priced postal code that differs from Stripe's address, or an order priced without one whose address is in a dearer zone, sets `orders.delivery_mismatch_at` and never changes the charge. `anonymise_order()` removes the delivery note and the mismatch event's codes; `forbid_change()` is patched to allow that removal. | 2026-10-14 | *Why:* the rows `orders.weight-or-price-rate-tables-zones-profiles`, `orders.local-pickup-at-own-location` and `orders.local-delivery` (all full). Migration versions: `20261014230143_shipping_methods`, `20261014230201_shipping_methods_rules`, `20261014230213_shipping_methods_copy`, `20261014230225_postal_points`, `20261014230237_shipping_plan_features`, applied by CI in the `release` job on 14 October 2026 at 23:54 UTC. Advisors checked after: no new security finding; performance INFO only (the new indexes not yet used). Still to do by a person: one paid order each (a method with a zone, priced pickup, local delivery) in Stripe test mode with the emails read, the GeoNames licence and attribution confirmed, and the legal review of spec section 8. A radius is measured between postal-code centres, so it is coarse inside cities. The owner kept `orders.local-delivery` at Full with that limit (15 October 2026): "inside the area" means a postal code whose centre is inside the circle, not a circle on a map, so a re-rate does not lower the row for it. |
| D238 | **Customer notifications, delivery tracking and staff alerts (wave 3).** `docs/wave-3-notifications.md` is the contract. **Notifications.** The order and return emails are one list (`NOTIFICATIONS`), each with a switch per event (`notification_settings`) asked through `notificationOn()`. The order confirmation, the renewal and change confirmations, the withdrawal acknowledgement and the return decisions are required, and the database refuses them off; the acknowledgement cannot be edited either. Owners write the subject, heading and intro per language (`email_templates`) with named placeholders only, checked by `templateProblems()` (placeholders, braces, any web address by its shape, `findClaims()`) and checked again at send. `fillTemplate()` fills them in the order's language and currency, falling back per part to the standard text. There is a preview with a sample order and a test email. Texts are copied with a store only when the owner ticks *Email texts* (copy group `emails`). A refund email switched off still lets its credit note go out. **Tracking.** Tracked parcels are polled every five minutes (`pollTracking()`, `shipment_tracking`; parcels stop being followed after 30 days and their events are cleared 90 days after that). Carrier codes are mapped to phases by `phaseOf()`: Bring verified; PostNord, Helthjem and Porterbuddy not yet, so they are shown only. Shoppers' order pages draw the stored rows. Out-for-delivery and delivered emails go once per parcel, off by default, are worded per parcel, and are hand-written only. A consignment of several packages is delivered only when every package is, from the last one (`parseTracking()`, `PACKAGE_DELIVERED`), so one package at the door never starts the withdrawal period. A verified delivered code records `delivered_at` only through `record_carrier_delivery()`: sent in full, every parcel delivered, never over a date, with `delivered_source = 'carrier'`. **Staff.** `store_events` rows, queued by triggers in the paying transaction (also for an order paid after it was cancelled) once per event, feed new-order and return-requested emails to the recipients the owner chose: members whose role may read them, or the contact address; owners by default. The new-order email shows what was charged and any venue balance. Refund-overdue and low-stock move to the same recipients. There is an optional daily or weekly summary from D152's figures. No staff email carries a shopper's name, email, phone or address. **Rules.** `when an order is paid and {conditions} then tag, notify or hold fulfilment` (`order_rules`, a closed list, at most 50), run once per event and logged in `order_rule_runs`. A held order cannot get a shipment (`shipments_not_held()`). | 2026-10-15 | *Why:* the rows `orders.editable-notification-email-templates`, `orders.delivery-status-notifications-tracking-page` and `orders.staff-alerts-for-new-orders-order-automation`, re-rated on 2026-10-15: the templates and staff-alert rows Full, the tracking row Partial until one real Bring parcel has been followed in production (every test reads Bring's answer from fixtures written from its documentation). Migration versions: `20261015001011_notifications`, `20261015001113_notifications_rules`, `20261015001242_notifications_copy`, `20261015001332_notifications_plan_features`, applied by CI in the `release` job on 15 October 2026 at 05:28 UTC (the first push's run stopped at a test of its own before applying anything). Advisors checked after: no new finding (the new tables RLS on with no policy, as every private table; new indexes not yet used). Still to do by a person: the legal review of spec section 8; verifying the PostNord, Helthjem and Porterbuddy codes; following one real Bring parcel to delivery in production. The owner kept `orders.delivery-status-notifications-tracking-page` at Partial (15 October 2026): it moves to Full only once one real Bring parcel, on a store's own Mybring agreement, has been followed by `pollTracking()` in production to a recorded `delivered_at` with `delivered_source = 'carrier'` and the withdrawal period starting from it. |
| D239 | **Exchanges and return labels (wave 3).** `docs/wave-3-exchanges.md` is the contract (3.14 records the review fixes). **Exchanges.** Staff add catalogue goods to a return as replacements (`return_exchanges`, `return_exchange_lines`). They are priced by `priceDraft()` in the original order's market view, with no order-level discount and an optional lower price per line. The server nets them against the return's credit, which is always `refundFor()`'s, into a refund due, an even exchange or a payment to collect. The customer confirms on `/account/exchange/{token}`. Staff may record the agreement for a voluntary return only; a withdrawal's proposal is emailed to the order's own address only, with no share link, and is refused once staff changed the order's email after the withdrawal (`email_changed`) (CRD Art. 13(1)). Processing makes a new order (`source = 'exchange'`, D141 sequence via `insertOrder()`) and only then holds its stock. The credit moves as a transfer refund on the original order and an `exchange` payment on the new one. The rest is refunded through `refundOrder()` (stock restored once, `restockIf`), paid by a link to Stripe's hosted page, or recorded as paid outside Kaizen. The original order gets one `return_exchange` credit note; the new order gets its own invoice. Bonus and affiliate reversals count `exchange` payments in the paid base. **Return labels.** Bring's return service is booked through `bookReturn()`, from the return's page or by the five-minute job for withdrawals (`label_auto`), one booking at a time. The shopper gets an email button and a status-page link to a route that fetches the label from Bring with the store's keys and never stores it. The cost is taken off only at refund, when staff confirm the label was used and the shopper pays, never above the cost and never for a test label. A failed booking leaves the pasted address. | 2026-10-15 | Rows `orders.exchanges` (missing to partial: legal review of section 8, one pay link paid in Stripe test mode, the euro scenario is in `exchanges.int.test.ts`, not `checkout-kinds.int.test.ts`; the once-shown share link is held by `ExchangeLinkHolder` so a refresh keeps it) and `orders.return-labels` (partial, bucket B: not yet booked with a real Mybring agreement). Migration versions: `20261015050012_exchanges`, `20261015050226_exchanges_rules`, `20261015065442_exchanges_plan_features`, `20261015083910_exchanges_bonus_base`, applied by CI in the `release` job on 15 October 2026 at 10:02 UTC (advisors checked after: no new finding). Not built: booking a label from the approve form (spec 2.3; staff book from the approved return's page); whether a label was used is staff's word at refund, not carrier tracking. Migration `kaizena_flow_rename` renames the platform's own data only: the template store and the platform's hidden stores ("Kaizena Flow Demo"), the template's "Made with Kaizena Flow" footer link and its address in every menu that copied it, the template store's pages, and the plan comparison's texts; Kaizena Flow's own pages are the platform admins' to edit. | 2026-10-15 | Asked for by the owner (rebranded to Kaizena Flow, `kaizenaflow.com`). | D288 | **What a payment was, Stripe's risk and disputes (wave 4 unit A).** `docs/wave-4-payments.md` 3 is the contract. A captured Stripe payment's charge is read once through the store's connected account (`readPaymentFacts()` in `after()` the payment, and the five-minute job for any of the last day still unread) and its method, wallet, card brand, Stripe's risk level and score and the charge id are kept on `commerce.payments` as Stripe gave them (`factsOfCharge()`); nothing is guessed, an unread payment is *Unknown*. The order page has a *Payment* card (the method, *Stripe: elevated risk* or *highest risk* with the score, each dispute with its reason, status, evidence deadline in the store's time zone and a link to the dispute in the store's Stripe Dashboard); the list shows *Disputed* and risk badges and filters by them (`pay=disputed`, `pay=elevated_risk`); an automation rule may hold an order by Stripe's risk (condition `risk`, never met while unread, the rule's facts read the charge first). Disputes are `commerce.payment_disputes`, written only by `applyStripeDispute()` from the connected account's `charge.dispute.*` events (added to `WEBHOOK_EVENTS`, so older endpoints get them through `ensureSubscriptionEvents()`), one row per dispute, its identity fixed and a closed dispute kept as it closed by a trigger, never deleted; a dispute on a payment the store does not have changes nothing. Staff are emailed once when one opens and once three days before its deadline (staff alert event `dispute`, owners by default and added to stores that saved their choices). A lost dispute is money out in the Finance bridge (*Lost disputes*, without VAT, with the fee where known, in the period it closed), never a refund. The Finance page lists *Payments by method* (with VAT, rows adding up to the money received, CSV `finance.payment_methods`). Finance's payment fees are Stripe's real fee for each order whose every captured Stripe payment has one in a currency the store converts, the estimate for the rest, and the label says *estimated* with how many are real; *Payments by method* likewise per payment. | 2026-10-19 | The owner: "Push it when the checks pass, then start unit C".
