| D292 | **The payment-provider adapter (wave 4 unit E).** `docs/wave-4-payments.md` 7 is the contract. E1 (built first, nothing changing for shoppers or staff): a provider's answer about a payment is one shape (`PaymentAnswer`) and one function decides what becomes of the order from it (`paymentFate()`, `applyPaymentAnswer()`), so Stripe and the next provider share the order's completion, cancellation, confirmation and every step after; the late read, closing open payments and refunds go to the payment's own provider through `PaymentProvider` (`refresh()`, `close()`, `refund()`), with Stripe's code moved behind it unchanged. E2 (Mollie): an owner connects the store's own Mollie account by approving Kaizena Flow at Mollie (OAuth, the refresh token encrypted in `mollie_connections`) and chooses Mollie per mode (`checkout_providers`); a cart's order then asks who pays and where it goes (Mollie's page asks neither), is paid on Mollie's hosted page through the Payments API with Kaizena Flow's fee as Mollie's application fee (capped at half the payment), offers what Mollie has active less pay-later methods and what the owner switched off for the country, and is answered by a webhook that only names the payment, which is always fetched from Mollie; refunds go to Mollie with an idempotency key and Mollie's refunds and chargebacks are recorded as Stripe's are. Subscriptions, deposits, hosts' listings, boxes and pay links stay on Stripe. Live Mollie payments wait for Kaizena Flow's Mollie app; everything is tested against a recorded fake of Mollie's API. Migration versions (applied by CI, 20 October 2026): `20261019223435_mollie`, `20261019223525_mollie_rules`. Advisors checked after: no new warning (the standing ones only); INFO only (RLS without policies on `checkout_providers` and `mollie_connections`, their `updated_by`/`connected_by` indexes not used yet). | 2026-10-19 | The owner: "start wave 4 and add Mollie" (the wave's units in order, `docs/wave-4-payments.md` 1). The spec allows E1 and E2 apart; moving Stripe first, with the integration tests of every payment path unchanged and green, keeps the move and the new provider in separate, checkable commits. |
| D293 | **Payment terms for company accounts (wave 4 unit F).** `docs/wave-4-payments.md` 8 is the contract. A company (D108) has payment terms (15, 30 or 60 days, or none) and an optional credit limit kept in the store's main currency, set on its page by staff with `customers:write` (audited `company.terms_changed`). Who may buy on terms is only `termsOffer()` (`src/lib/payment-terms.ts`): the business feature on, a signed-in owner or employee of an active company with terms, goods bought once only, something to pay, and the limit (what the company already owes plus this order, converted at the store's rates, a currency with no rate refused) not passed. The cart offers *Pay by invoice* beside paying now, asking who pays with the billing address and showing the store's terms of sale (recorded before the order is confirmed). The order is confirmed as a venue booking is (D66): `placeOrder({ terms })` checks again under a lock on the company row and writes `order_payment_terms` (due day = the store's day plus the days) and the whole total as the balance; `confirmOnTerms()` adds a pending `terms` payment (never money: the order page's key and what a cancellation voids) and completes the order, so it is `paid` as a shop status, its stock drawn and its invoice issued with a `due` line (due date, terms, the supply date the order day). It is not a sale until its money arrives: *Pay now* on the order page opens Stripe's hosted page for the balance (`startTermsPayment()`, `applyPaymentAnswer()`'s `terms` purpose, `settleTerms()`), or staff record it paid outside Kaizena Flow (`recordTermsPaidOutside()`, D173's rules and cash ceilings). Cancelled before it is paid, the invoice gets a credit note through a `void` refund (no money, no provider, no refund in any figure, no integration event) and the goods go back in stock. The five-minute job sends the reminder three days before and the notice the day after (`order.payment_due`, `order.payment_overdue`, switchable), and the staff alert *Payment overdue* (owners by default). The control center says how many are overdue, the Orders list filters `terms_due`/`terms_overdue`, and Finance shows what is owed today (docs/analytics.md *Owed on payment terms*). A balance on terms earns no bonus or referral reward. The customer words are hand-written in nb, sv, da and en (`HAND_WRITTEN_ONLY`) and need legal review. Not built: part payments, *Pay now* for a store on Mollie alone, writing off a sent order that is never paid. Migration versions (applied by CI, 20 October 2026): `20261020001515_payment_terms`, `20261020001647_payment_terms_rules`; `20261020091606_payment_terms_order_index` (applied by CI the same day, with D294) for the advisor's one new finding (the order key without a covering index), which it cleared. Advisors otherwise: INFO only (RLS without policies on `order_payment_terms`, as every commerce table; its new indexes not used yet). | 2026-10-20 | The owner: "Go with your recommendations, finish payment terms first". The spec's new status `awaiting_payment` was not made: `status = 'paid'` is read in about a hundred places, and the venue's model already carries a balance owed on a confirmed order; a sale is still only a captured payment (`PAID`), dated by the order day like every order (a held card captured later is the same, D289), so a closed period grows when an order placed in it is paid. The row `checkout.b2b-payment-terms` is Partial for that and for the missing browser test. |
| D294 | **Nested columns, one level deep.** A column can hold a *Columns* component (the Components tab's *Columns*, two columns to start), whose columns are columns like a row's, with their own settings, lines and widths; a nested column holds any component but another Columns. It is a block, so text and pictures can sit above and below it in the same column, and everything that walks a page (styles, fonts, pictures, translation, visibility, templates, copies, forms, motion, A/B parts, design profiles, page rules) sees what it holds. The canvas reuses the row's column drawing and its edits: a component dragged into a nested column goes there, a row's column dragged onto a nested column becomes one of its columns, a nested column dragged out goes back to the row, and the *New line* strips and dividers work inside it as in a row. A column holding a Columns component never moves into one (that would nest two levels), and the component goes when its last column leaves. Nesting by dragging (asked for after the first release, where only the Columns component nested): while a column, the *Column* tile or a row's layout from the Rows tab is dragged, every column of the page that is not itself nested shows a *Nest columns here* strip above and below its components; dropped there, the column moves into a new Columns component at that place (`nestColumn()`, its old row or component going when left empty), the tile brings one new column and a layout its columns (`newColumnsBlock()`). The builder measures its drop targets throughout a drag (`MeasuringStrategy.Always`), since strips that appear when a drag starts move what is below them. With it, a fix for dragging columns in Kaizena Flow's own pages: a column's tools (its drag handle among them) now show whenever anything in the column is pointed at, at its top-right. Before, only the innermost part under the pointer showed its tools, so a column whose first component starts at its top edge (as Kaizena Flow's columns do, with no padding of their own) showed that component's tools in the very place of its own, and its handle could not be reached to drag it onto a new line. | 2026-10-20 | The owner: "a column should be able to nest a subset of columns", for all builders. A component rather than a column of columns, as Beaver Builder does, so a column keeps its own components around the nested ones and no reader of `column.blocks` breaks; the column edits run on the component as a row (`held()`), so nested columns behave exactly as a row's without a second set of edits. |

| D297 | **Broadcasts (wave 5 unit C, `docs/wave-5-marketing.md` 5).** A store writes an email once, per language, from blocks (heading, text, button, a picture from its own media library as JPEG, PNG or GIF, a line; `broadcastContentSchema`, `src/lib/broadcasts.ts`), and sends it to the addresses on its subscriber list that are `subscribed`: everyone, or a segment's (D296's `segmentPeople()` and `matches()` at the start). `commerce.broadcasts` moves only draft → scheduled or sending, scheduled → draft or sending, sending → sent or cancelled (`broadcasts_rules()`); its words and audience are fixed once a send starts, and only a draft is deleted. | D299 | **Discounts in depth (wave 5 unit E, `docs/wave-5-marketing.md` 7), built in four steps; E1 codes in depth.** A code (`discount_codes`) may reach products in chosen categories and tags (`term_ids`, matched through `termsOfProducts()` like campaigns, `reaches()`) as well as chosen products, ask for a minimum number of items (`min_quantity`, 1 to 1,000, units of the lines it reaches that are bought once and cost something today: `minimum_quantity`), and be for customer groups (`tier_ids`) or up to 100 named customers (`customer_ids`, typed as emails and kept as ids): a guest is asked to sign in, anyone else gets `not_eligible` (hand-written nb, sv, da, en). A code may hand out single-use codes instead (`unique_codes`; its own text is then no code): `discount_code_items`, made by `generateCodes()` (1 to 10,000 at a time, 100,000 per code, a prefix and eight characters without look-alikes from `randomBytes()`, never the text of another code or generated code of the store: unique index and the trigger `discount_code_text_unique()`), each used by one order not cancelled (`orders.discount_code_item_id`, counted under the item's row lock in `placeOrder()`; the code's own limits do not apply to them), and downloaded as a CSV (code, used, made on) from the code's page (`marketing:write`, audited `discount.codes_generated`/`codes_exported` with counts only). The order keeps the generated text as its `discount_code`. A store's copy carries the new settings with its own categories and groups, never its customers (a code for named customers is copied switched off) or generated codes (`never`). Steps to come: several codes per order under each code's combination choice (E2), automatic amount off, free shipping and tiers (E3), buy X get Y (E4). **E2, several codes on one order (2026-10-21):** a code is of one class (`codeClass()`: `shipping` for free shipping, `products` for one naming products, categories or tags, `order` for one on everything) and says which classes it may be used with (`discount_codes.combines_with`, empty by default, so every code there today keeps working alone); two are used together only when each allows the other's class (`combines()`). The cart holds up to five codes (`carts.discount_code` the first, `more_codes` the rest; applying adds, a sixth is `codes_full`), each removed by itself. Which apply is only `stackCodes()` (pure): of every set whose codes all combine, applied in turn (product codes, then order codes, then free shipping, each counting what the ones before it left, no line below zero) and all still applying, the one taking most off, the first typed winning a tie; every other code says why (its own reason, `not_combinable` with the code it cannot be used with, or its reason beside the others). `stackForBasket()` (all codes locked first, in id order) is what `cartSummary()`, the checkout's code field and `placeOrder()` use; a code that fails on its own still stops the checkout, one left out beside a better one does not. The order keeps the codes used joined by ` + ` in `discount_code` (so invoices, emails, exports and Stripe's one coupon need nothing new), the first's ids, every code in `order_discount_codes` with what it took off (fixed by a trigger), and every code typed in `entered_codes` (the changed-cart check compares those). A code's uses and a generated code's use count its places in `order_discount_codes` too; the analytics coupon table counts an order under each of its codes, the discount split by what each took off. `duplicate_store()` carries `combines_with` (patched in place); `order_discount_codes` is `never` in `COPY_RULES` and in `NOT_PERSONAL`. Words `m.codeProblems.not_combinable` and `codes_full`, nb, sv, da and en by hand; the editor's *Using it with other codes*. **E3, campaigns in depth (2026-10-21):** three more kinds of campaign, worked out by the same `applyCampaigns()` in the cart and in `placeOrder()`. An **amount** off (`campaigns.amounts`, per country in its own currency, optionally over a threshold) competes with the percentages and the "3 for 2" for the lines still free, split over the lines it reaches by what each costs (`splitAmount()`, largest remainder, never more than a line costs); it does not stack. A **tiered** campaign (`tier_basis` `quantity` or `spend`, two to five `tiers`, each step asking for more and giving more) takes the percentage of the highest step its reached lines come to (`tierPercent()`) and may stack (it then counts its lines before any reduction). **Free shipping** (optionally over a threshold, counted after the reductions) is decided after the price campaigns and gifts, the oldest that qualifies, takes the order's whole first shipping before codes, never with a subscription; it is part of the order's `discount_minor` and `campaign_discount_minor` (so the invoice's campaign row and the shipping's VAT follow as a free-shipping code's do) and kept as `orders.campaign_shipping` (`{ id, name, minor }`, a check holds it within the campaign discount); a campaign's uses and what it gave count it beside the lines' parts (`ordersOf()`). Amounts reach the cart in the currency shown (`shownIn()`). `nextSteps()` says how far a campaign's next step is (a tier's next step, an amount's or free shipping's threshold), only where no other campaign holds the lines; the cart shows it (`nextStepText()`). Notices say an amount, free shipping and a tier's ladder (`m.campaigns.*`, nb, sv, da, en by hand); cards carry only percentages, "3 for 2" and *Up to n % off*. `duplicate_store()` carries the new columns (patched in place). **E4, buy X get Y (2026-10-21):** a campaign of kind `bxgy`: its reach is the buy set, one use asks for `buy_min_quantity` units or `buy_min_amounts` per country (exactly one), the get set is `get_product_ids`/`get_term_ids` (both empty: the buy set), and each use gives up to `get_quantity` units `free`, at `get_percent` or `get_amounts` off each (never more than a unit costs), at most `uses_per_order` times. `bxgyOff()` (pure) counts the dearest unused units of the buy set as bought and gives the cheapest unused units of the get set, never one counted as bought; a use that gives nothing does not count. It competes for lines with the other price campaigns, takes every line it counted or gave, does not stack, and the shopper adds the given units themselves; the order keeps it on the given lines' campaign parts. Notices on the products of both sets (*Kjøp 2, få 1 gratis*, `m.campaigns.bxgy*`, nb, sv, da and en by hand), on cards only when it needs no amount. The database check is one `campaigns_bxgy` wrapped in `coalesce(…, false)` (a null would pass a check). `duplicate_store()` carries the columns with the get set remapped and switches off a copy whose get set lost everything (patched in place). | 2026-10-20 | The owner: "Unit E next." Four steps, because each touches what a cart costs and must keep `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()` together in a NOK and a euro scenario. | D300 | **Gift cards (wave 5 unit F, `docs/wave-5-marketing.md` 8), built in four steps; F1 the model.** Multi-purpose vouchers only (Directive 2016/1065: no VAT when sold, the goods carry theirs when it is spent), flagged for an accountant's and a lawyer's look. `commerce.gift_cards` (store, currency fixed, the amount issued, `source` `order` with its order line and unit, or `staff` with who issued it, status `active` or `deactivated`, `test_mode`, the customer and recipient, `send_at`/`sent_at`, `expires_at` from `gift_card_settings.expiry_months`, default 36, 12 to 120 or none) keeps no code: `code_hash` is the HMAC of the normalised code with a key made from `SETTINGS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` for this use alone (`src/server/gift-card-codes.ts`), `last4` what people see; codes are 16 characters of the generated codes' alphabet (`src/lib/gift-cards.ts`, free of zod for the checkout). A card's balance is only `commerce.gift_card_balance()` over `gift_card_entries` (`issue`, `hold`, `release`, `spend`, `refund`, `adjust`, `expire`, `void`), append-only, never below zero, written only by `gift_card_issue()` (once per unit of a line), `gift_card_hold()` (active, unexpired, with its code, its own currency and mode, the balance for it), `gift_card_release()`, `gift_card_spend()`, `gift_card_refund()` (up to what the order spent on it, also after expiry), `gift_card_adjust()` (with a reason), `gift_card_expire()` and `gift_card_void()`, each once per key under the card's lock; triggers refuse any other writer, a change to a card's amount, currency, source or dates, a second code, reactivating, a second send and any change to who it is for but clearing it. The database allows the store feature `gift_cards` (needs the shop); its switch reaches the Features page with F2. All three tables `never`/`settings` in `COPY_RULES`, the cards personal data (erasure clears the buyer and recipient, keeps the card and its money). **F2, selling one:** a product of kind `gift_card` (offered only while the feature `gift_cards` is on, `kind_offered()`; its variants its amounts, each `digital`; the VAT category `voucher`, 0 % in every country through `set_vat_rate()`, unverified until an accountant reads it, and only on gift cards, both held at commit by `check_gift_card_product()`), bought in the cart like goods; *Send it to someone* on the product page (`GiftCardBuy`) puts who it is for, from whom, a message and the day (today to a year, `cleanGiftCardRecipient()`, the gift message's limits, refused not cut) on the cart line, which `giftCardKey()` names so two people are two lines, and `placeOrder()` freezes them on the order line. Nothing comes off a gift card and it counts towards no threshold (campaigns, the group's and the friend's discounts, codes, credits, free shipping), in `cartSummary()` and `placeOrder()` alike (`voucher`); it asks no tick for downloads and is no download. The cards are issued by the database, one per unit, once the order has a captured payment and is paid (`issue_order_gift_cards()` from deferred triggers on payments and on the order, whichever comes last; never a copy's or a host's; a test payment's are test cards), with no code; `deliverGiftCards()` (`src/server/gift-card-delivery.ts`, after the order's confirmation and from the every-minute marketing job) makes each code, keeps its hash (`gift_card_set_code()`, again only while the card was never delivered, so a failed email never leaves a card no one can use) and emails it (`gift_card.issued`, required and locked; `sendEmail()`'s `secrets` keep `[code]` in the log) to the buyer at once or to the person it is for from 08:00 on their day, the buyer told it went (`gift_card.on_its_way`), then marks it sent once (`gift_card_mark_sent()`). A gift card is withdrawn from within 14 days of the purchase, card by card while a card is whole (`lineEligibility()`'s `giftCard`, refusal `gift_card_spent`), and the withdrawal voids those cards with the order (`gift_card_withdraw()`); an order anonymised clears who its cards were for (`orders_gift_card_people()`). The demo `Demo: Gavekort` (500 and 1 000 kr) is the template's; a new store's copy waits as a draft, as every digital product does. Words are hand-written nb, sv, da and en (`m.giftCard` for the browser, `m.giftCardLine`, `m.giftCardEmail`; the withdrawal sentence and the email `HAND_WRITTEN_ONLY`), flagged for legal review. Not yet: spending a card (F3, so a store should not switch gift cards on before it), the invoice line's own words and Finance leaving vouchers out of sales (F4). **F3, spending one:** a *Gift card* field beside the discount code at the cart and at checkout (`GiftCardForm`; never the code's field: a card is money, a code is a discount) finds a card by its code's hash (`findGiftCard()`: the store's own, active, delivered, unexpired, of the store's mode, with a balance; any other answer is *This gift card can't be used*, never whether it exists, except another currency, which is named) and keeps it on the cart by its id alone (`cart_gift_cards`, at most three, in the order added; the code is never kept), each try counted per cart (10 an hour) and per client (20, the keyed address hash VIES uses) in `chat_usage` (`giftcard:*`). What the cards pay is only `giftCardsPay()` (pure: in the order added, each up to its balance, up to what is due online without the order's own gift cards, leaving at least the provider's smallest charge when anything is left), used by `cartSummary()` (`giftCards`, `dueNowMinor` after them) and `placeOrder()` alike; never for a subscription, a host's listing or an order on terms. A card pays as a payment, never a discount: the total and VAT are unchanged, and `placeOrder()` writes a pending `payments` row of provider `gift_card` per card (`gift_card_id`; its test mode is the store's, as a venue payment's), which the database holds on the card as it is written (`payments_gift_card()`: never more than the order's total online without its gift cards, never for a change or an exchange, its card, order and amount fixed), spends when the order is paid and releases when it is cancelled unpaid (`orders_gift_card_payments()`; an order paid after it was cancelled does not take its cards again and records `gift_card.unpaid` for staff). Stripe (or Mollie) is asked only for the rest: one line *Order N* holding the shipping, no coupon. An order the cards pay in full has no payment page: the cart asks who pays, as for Mollie, with the store's terms of sale (and their tick in `checkbox` mode), and `confirmPaidByGiftCards()` completes it with the first card's payment reference as the order page's key (added to every list that finds an order by its key); a card typed at checkout that pays it all sends the shopper back to the cart for that. A refund goes back where the money came from, the shopper's money first and the cards last (`refundOrder()`: a card's part is recorded `succeeded` with no provider call, capped by `refunds_manual_cap()`), and the trigger `refunds_gift_card()` writes it on the card, by any refund path, also after expiry. The invoice names what the cards paid (`paid_gift_card`, with each card's last four, `invoice-text.ts` `paidGiftCard`), the rest paid online; a test card's order is a test (`invoice_eligibility()`); the bonus and referral refund bases count a card's part as paid; the payments report calls it *Gift card*. Words are `m.giftCardPay` (nb, sv, da, en by hand). Not yet: an order changed or exchanged after it was paid with a card refunds through its own paths without the card (the order edit's and exchange's refundable sums leave it out), and a card added on the cart page after checkout began is taken only when checkout starts again (the open checkout does not compare cards); F4 has the owner's screens and the figures. **F4, the owner's screens and figures:** Products → Gift cards (`/admin/{store}/gift-cards`, `products:read`, kept while the feature is off: the cards are customers' money) lists what customers hold now per currency and the cards by state (`cardState()`: not sent yet, active, spent, expired, deactivated; spent and expired read from the balance and the date, never stored), searched by the last four, an order number or, for staff who may read customers, the person; a card's page shows its ledger in words and the orders it paid for. *Issue a gift card* (`products:write`, the Products section's, as every change on these pages; `issueGiftCard()`) makes a card in a currency the store sells in and in the store's payment mode, shows its code once (marked delivered there and then, so the code is fixed; emailed to the person it is for when an address is given, `sendIssuedCard()`), never keeping or logging it; *Change the balance* (`adjustGiftCard()`, a reason, never below zero) and *Deactivate* (`deactivateGiftCard()`) go through the ledger's functions; a bought card whose email failed can be sent again (`deliverGiftCards({ cardId })`), and a delivered code can never be sent again (only its hash is kept: deactivate and issue a new one). How long new cards last is the settings card (`products:write`, 12 to 120 months or never). The daily job expires what is left on a card past its date, once (`expireGiftCards()`). Finance leaves gift cards sold out of its sales (`GIFT_CARD_LINE`/`VOUCHER_NET` in `analytics-sql.ts`: out of revenue, gross sales, units and cost coverage; an order of gift cards only is no order; a refund is scaled by what of the order is not gift cards; what gift cards paid is no card payment to estimate a fee on), counts what they buy, and shows a *Gift cards* section (`giftCardLiability()`, `GiftCardsView`): held now and the period's issued, spent, refunded back, changed by staff, expired and deactivated, per currency, never converted or added across currencies, the cards of the store's payment mode now, CSV `finance.gift_cards` (a currency per row). The invoice marks a gift card line (`voucher` on the snapshot's line, in SQL and in `buildInvoiceSnapshot()`) and says *Gift card: a multi-purpose voucher, VAT is charged when it is spent* (`invoice-text.ts` `voucherLine`, nb, sv, da, en); invoices issued before keep their snapshot. The AI manager's `gift_card_overview` (read-only, `products:read`) gives counts and what customers hold per currency, never a code or a person. Audit actions are `gift_card.*` (area products) with ids and amounts. Not yet: a custom amount the shopper chooses, opening and closing balances on the liability report, refunds of order changes and exchanges back to a card. | 2026-10-21 | The owner: "Then start unit F." | D301 | **Product reviews (wave 5 unit G, `docs/wave-5-marketing.md` 9), built in four steps; G1 the model and writing, G2 checking and showing.** A review is a buyer's own stars (1 to 5) and words about a product they bought and received in the store, so every written review is a verified purchase by construction; an imported one is marked imported and never verified. `commerce.product_reviews` (store, product, variant, `source` `buyer` with its order, order line and `buyer_key` (`c:{customer}`, or for G3's guests `e:` and the hash of the order's email), or `import` with where and when it was written; the name shown, stars, title, text, language, `status` `pending`, `published` or `hidden` with `hidden_reason` (`not_about_product`, `offensive`, `personal_data`, `spam`, `other`), the store's `answer`); one review per order line and one per product and buyer. The database's `product_reviews_rules()`: a buyer's line must be of that order and of a variant of that product; who and what it is about are fixed; the stars and words change only inside the author's write (`kaizen.review_author`), which only `src/server/reviews.ts` sets, so staff can never change a word or a star; the state moves `pending → published | hidden`, `published ↔ hidden`, and back to `pending` only in the author's write; a review is deleted only where `kaizen.review_delete` is set (the author, erasure) or by its product's or customer's own deletion. What can be reviewed is only `reviewableLines()`: a sold order of the signed-in customer's (D298's `SOLD`), a variant of a product that is not a gift card, received (goods when the order has `delivered_at`, a download once paid, a service when its booking ended, confirmed and not a no-show) within 365 days; the newest receipt of each product. *Reviews* in My account (`/account/reviews`, *Write a review* beside each such line of an order) writes and edits through `writeReview()` (checked again under the line's lock, 20 writes a customer a day in `chat_usage`, words cleaned by `cleanReview()`, refused not cut) and deletes with `deleteOwnReview()`. The store's `review_settings.publish`: `after_check` (default, a new or edited review waits) or `at_once` (published when written; an edit of a shown one stays shown, a hidden one is checked again). Feature `reviews` (Customers, needs the shop, off by default; the Features page counts twelve). **G2:** Products → Reviews (`/admin/{store}/reviews`, `products:read`; publish, hide with a reason (*Hiding a review because it is negative is not allowed*), answer and the setting `products:write`; `src/server/reviews-admin.ts`; audit `review.published`, `review.hidden`, `review.answered`, `review.settings`, area products) with the queue first. The product parts `rating` (stars, average to one decimal, the count, linking down) and `reviews` (average, how many gave each number of stars, the newest ten with *Show more* from `/api/reviews`, *How reviews are checked*) draw only with a published review (`productPartShows()`), from the cached `reviewFacts()` (tag `reviews:{store}`, refreshed by every decision, by an author's write that changes what is shown and by erasure); both are in the default layout and the builder's palette while the feature is on. The product's JSON-LD carries `aggregateRating` and the newest five `review`s only with a published review. Review words are text in their own language, never HTML and never translated. Personal data: `product_reviews` (export section `reviews`, erasure deletes); `never` copied, `review_settings` copied as settings. Shopper words are `m.reviews` (nb, sv, da, en by hand; the account intro and *How reviews are checked* `HAND_WRITTEN_ONLY`), flagged for legal review (Directive 2005/29/EC Art. 7(6), Annex I 23b and 23c). Not yet: request emails, imports, the AI manager's tool and the control center (G3), pictures (G4), stars on product cards. **G3a (24 October):** review request emails. Products → Reviews gains *Ask buyers for a review* (`requests_on`, `request_days` 3 to 60, `saveRequestSettings()`; switching on sets `requests_enabled_at`, staying on keeps it, `products:write`, audit `review.settings`). `runReviewRequests()` (`src/server/review-requests.ts`, the every-minute marketing job after the automations) asks each sold order (D298's `SOLD`, not a gift card, never a copy) once, when its newest received reviewable line was received the days ago, after the switch and within D298's `CATCH_UP_DAYS`, with something still unreviewed by the buyer, **only when the order's address is `subscribed`** (asking for a review is marketing, BGH VI ZR 225/17): one `commerce.review_requests` row per order (claimed before the email, the hash of a 32-byte token, 90 days, the outcome; one table where the spec named two, `review_invitations` folded in), then the email through `marketingEmail()` (the subscriber's unsubscribe link and `List-Unsubscribe` headers, the order's language and market, kind `marketing.review_request`, counted in `MARKETING_EMAIL_KINDS` against the plan's month), naming up to five products. Its link `/account/review/{token}` (a no-extras path, `isReviewLinkPath()`) lists the order's reviewable lines and writes them without signing in through `writeReviewAs()` with an `invitation` `Reviewer` (`reviewableLinesFor()` for that order only; the buyer key the order's account's, else `e:` the hash of the order's address, as erasure finds it); an unknown, other store's or spent link says the same. Rows go on the buyer's erasure and after 400 days (`pruneReviewRequests()`, daily); personal-data register `review_requests` (export section emails), `never` copied. *How reviews are checked* now says buyers review from their account or the store's emailed link (four languages). Not a D165 job, unlike the spec: a review file is small, has no pictures to fetch and is read in one request, as the 404 report's and analytics' files are. *Download as CSV* (POST `/admin/{store}/reviews/export`, `products:read`, same site) writes the published and waiting reviews through `writeCsv()` with whether each is verified; hidden ones stay out. The AI manager's `reviews_overview` (`products:read`, feature `reviews`): counts by state, how many imported, the average and the best and worst ten products with at least three published reviews, averaged in code, never an author or a review's words. The control center says *n reviews are waiting to be checked* (`reviewsWaiting`, for members who may read products, with the feature on). The admin's words for reviews are now plain templates and `{ one, many }` pairs (no functions), as the catalogues are. **G4 (26 October):** pictures in reviews. A buyer adds up to three to their review in its form: each made smaller in the browser by D213's `compressPicture()` without AVIF (1600 px, tried again at 1280 and 1024 px while over 600 KB, and a 480 px copy), sent with the review and checked again on the server from its bytes by `checkPicture()` (`src/lib/review-pictures.ts`: WebP, or JPEG from a browser that cannot write WebP, the two of one kind, 600 KB and 150 KB, 1600 and 480 px). `commerce.review_pictures` (review, place 0 to 2, kind, size, bytes, `hidden_at`/`hidden_by`; the database's `review_pictures_rules()`: added and taken out only inside the review author's write, on a buyer's review, never hidden from the start, otherwise only hidden or shown, gone with the review). `writeReviewAs()` checks them before anything is written, keeps or takes out the review's own (`keep`) and adds the new ones at the free places, uploading their files inside the transaction (a refused upload undoes the whole write); a store without the bucket takes none. The product page draws the shown pictures under each review as small copies that open the picture. The files go after the rows: an edit that takes one out, the author's delete and erasure remove them; the daily job sweeps files no row names (older than an hour). Personal data `review_pictures` and `storage:review-pictures` (export section `reviews`, each picture by its address), `never` copied. Words `m.reviews.picture*` (nb, sv, da, en by hand). Migrations `20261026145501_review_pictures`, `20261026145526_review_pictures_rules` (applied by CI run 40348477059, 26 October 2026; the table with RLS, its rules trigger and the private bucket confirmed in production; advisors: no new warning, INFO only). | 2026-10-21 | The owner: "Start unit G." | D316 | **A font Kaizena Flow cannot serve is only the fallback font.** React holds a streamed `<Suspense>` boundary until the stylesheets linked with `precedence` have loaded (`$RR`) and gives the boundary up when one fails: the browser draws it again, the server's copy stays in its hidden segment, and the page holds its content twice (React error 419, "Switched to client rendering"). `/api/fonts/css/{slug}` (D59) therefore never fails a stylesheet a page links: every slug of a family name's shape (`FONT_SLUG`) is answered with the installed stylesheet (a year, `immutable`, as before) or, when there is none (never installed, kept empty), an empty stylesheet kept a minute, and, when the database does not answer, one not kept at all; a name of another shape is a 404 and `<FontLinks>` links none. `fontCss()` keeps a miss for seconds rather than days, so a family installed after a page asked for it is served at once, and a stylesheet kept empty counts as not installed, so `installFont()` fetches the family again and replaces it. `e2e/missing-font.spec.ts` holds a store page whose streamed headings name an empty and a missing font, and the design preview with an empty one: each draws once, `main` visible, with no page error or failed font request. | 2026-10-16 | Found on the public design preview with a font row whose stylesheet was empty (two canvases, `main` hidden in the theme store's frame); D255's stand-in stylesheets in the signed-in specs worked around it. Integration tests that seed fonts to avoid Google give them a stand-in stylesheet, since an empty one is installed again. No migration. |

Raw SQL reads move to `typedRows()`/`typedRow()` (`src/server/typed-rows.ts`: zod shapes per column, an error names the column and never the value), starting with the gift-card figures. The AI agents (the chat agent and the AI manager) have an eval (`src/lib/agent-eval.ts`, `src/server/agent-eval.ts`): nine cases (ten since the support tools) with the real prompts and tools, a scripted tool layer that runs nothing (gated calls answer *kept*), and a pass only when every case passes. Advisors checked after: the new table shows only the expected `rls_enabled_no_policy` notice. Two warnings were there before: `public.rls_auto_enable()` executable by anon and authenticated (a dashboard setting, not in the repository; it returns `event_trigger`, so it cannot be called directly), and leaked-password protection off. Both are in the follow-ups. Then `20261024200331_ai_eval_runs`, `20261024200349_ai_eval_runs_rls` (applied by CI 24 October 2026 20:50 UTC; advisors after: nothing new). The AI manager can now schedule (AGT-04, AGT-11). `start_experiment` takes an optional `at` (the store's wall-clock time) and then schedules the test through `scheduleExperiment()`, with the same checks. Follow-ups (`follow_up`, `list_follow_ups`, `cancel_follow_up` in `MANAGER_TOOLS`; `src/lib/follow-ups.ts`, `src/server/follow-ups.ts`, `src/server/follow-up-runner.ts`, `commerce.assistant_follow_ups`) give a task back to the AI manager at its time, as a turn in the conversation it was asked in. The turn runs in the person's name with what their role may do that day, sets no further follow-up and decides nothing in words. The person gets an email (`assistant.follow_up`) saying where to read the answer, never the answer itself. A follow-up can repeat daily or weekly at the same local time, and a failing one stops after three tries. Migration versions: `20261024204840_assistant_follow_ups`, `20261024204856_assistant_follow_ups_rls` (applied by CI 24 October 2026 23:06 UTC). Task parity, marketing first (AGT-01): the AI manager lists, drafts and sends or schedules broadcasts (the send waits for a yes), counts segments, tags customers, and lists and switches email automations (the switch waits for a yes), each through the screen's own function. Review moderation gets no tool: a review's words never go to a model (D301), so an agent could not judge one. Turns started by events (AGT-04): the order rule action *Ask the AI manager* queues a follow-up for the paid order, in the name of whoever last saved the rule while they are an owner, at most 20 a store a day. Restores (REL-04): `docs/runbooks/restore.md` has the targets and the copy's scheduled jobs as the first step, and `scripts/restore-check.mjs` runs the drill's checks read-only. `public.rls_auto_enable()` is no longer executable by `anon` and `authenticated`; its event trigger still fires, tested. Advisors after: the `rls_auto_enable` warning is gone (EXECUTE only for `postgres` and `service_role`); the new tables show only the expected `rls_enabled_no_policy` and `unused_index` notices; leaked-password protection is still off (a dashboard setting). Answering support (AGT-01): `get_support_conversation` reads a conversation as the inbox does (no partner's help, no contact details; an eval case hides an instruction in a transcript), `add_support_note` and `end_support_conversation` act as the person, and `reply_to_support` (gate `send`, the exact words approved, the claims filter first) takes the conversation over and answers in their name, all through `agentAct()`. This revises 5.5's "never transcripts" for a member with `support:read`, who reads them in the inbox anyway. The team and the plan (AGT-01): `list_staff`, `invite_staff`, `change_staff_role`, `remove_staff`, `billing_overview`, `change_plan` and `end_plan`, the owner's alone, through the Staff and Billing pages' functions with the membership read again; the team's changes wait for a yes under a new gate category, `access` (migration `20261025091235_approvals_access`, applied by CI 25 October 2026 09:56 UTC; advisors after: nothing new), the plan's under `spend`; the first plan is never chosen by a tool. Work (AGT-01): `work_overview`, `list_work_clients`, `list_work_assignments`, `log_time` and `issue_work_invoice` (send: issued through Work's own issue and emailed), only while the store has Work on. ### Selling from Norway to Sweden and Denmark

Norway is in the EEA but outside the EU's customs and VAT union, so a Norwegian
company shipping from Norway sells to Swedish and Danish customers as an
exporter. In practice:

- **Norwegian VAT (MVA).** Register once sales in Norway pass NOK 50,000 in 12
  months, and charge Norwegian VAT on Norwegian orders.
- **EU VAT on low-value parcels.** For orders worth up to €150, the store can
  register for the EU's Import One-Stop Shop (IOSS) and charge Swedish or
  Danish VAT (25% for standard goods) at checkout. Parcels then clear customs
  without the customer paying anything on delivery. To confirm with an
  accountant: Norway's VAT cooperation agreement with the EU is understood to let
  Norwegian companies register for IOSS without an EU intermediary.
- **Without IOSS**, the carrier collects VAT plus a handling fee from the
  customer on delivery. That is legal but a known reason for refused parcels
  and lost repeat customers.
- **EU customs duty.** Since 1 July 2026 the EU charges a flat €3 duty per item
  category on low-value parcels, which IOSS does not collect. Orders over €150
  pay normal duty and import VAT. The store should show these costs at checkout
  or ship duties-paid.
- **Customs declarations.** Every parcel needs a Norwegian export and an EU
  import declaration, normally filed by the carrier (Posten/Bring, PostNord)
  from the tariff code and country of origin now stored per variant.
- **Later option: stock in the EU.** With a warehouse in Sweden, EU sales
  become domestic and intra-EU: no customs per parcel, but a Swedish VAT
  registration and the EU One-Stop Shop. Worth reconsidering as EU volume grows.
- **Consumer law.** Norwegian customers are covered by angrerettloven and
  forbrukerkjøpsloven (complaints up to five years for goods meant to last).
  Swedish and Danish customers are covered by EU consumer law, including the
  withdrawal button and the 30-day prior-price rule. The store follows the
  stricter rule wherever the two differ.
- **Product safety.** Products sold to Swedish and Danish consumers fall under
  the EU General Product Safety Regulation, which needs an EU-based responsible
  person when the manufacturer is not in the EU. Norway is not in the EU for
  this purpose unless the regulation has been taken into the EEA Agreement
  (unverified).
- **To verify with Stripe:** that Stripe Tax covers Norwegian VAT and IOSS, and
  that Vipps MobilePay is available.

### Migration versions

On 24 September 2026 Kaizen became multi-tenant (see [`platform.md`](platform.md)).
The single-store migrations were replaced by a fresh baseline. In production,
the old schema was not dropped: it was renamed to `commerce_legacy` and can be
removed once the platform schema has proved itself. Supabase's migration
history therefore still lists the nine single-store migrations, then:

The template store was seeded from `supabase/seed.sql`, and the existing owner
account was carried over as platform admin and owner of the template store.

Supabase records each migration with the version it was applied at, not the
timestamp in the file name. This does not matter while migrations are applied
through Supabase's API. If the Supabase CLI is adopted later (for example for
branching), run `supabase migration repair` once to align the history.

## Open

### Required before the first sale

These come with selling online in these countries, whatever the platform:
WooCommerce and Shopify need them too.

| # | Item | Owner | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Norwegian VAT (MVA) registration | You | Once Norwegian sales pass NOK 50,000 in 12 months. |
| R2 | Decide how EU VAT is collected: register for IOSS, or let the carrier collect from customers on delivery | You (+ accountant) | IOSS is the better customer experience. See above. |
| R3 | Terms of sale, withdrawal information and privacy policy in Norwegian, Swedish and Danish | Claude drafts, you review | Built into Phase 1. |
| R4 | Packaging producer responsibility in each market | You | Check whether your volumes trigger registration in Norway, Sweden and Denmark; record numbers in `commerce.producer_registrations` (per store). |
| R5 | Product-safety contact details per manufacturer, and an EU responsible person where needed | You | The database will not activate a product without them. |
| R6 | Check the standard VAT rates in `commerce.countries` and decide on Stripe Tax for reduced rates (food, books, children's clothing) | You (+ accountant) | Orders show VAT at the standard rate of the shopper's country until then. |
| R7 | Turn on customer receipts in Stripe (Settings → Customer emails → Successful payments) | Each store owner | Optional since D26: Kaizen sends its own order confirmation once Resend is set up; Stripe's receipt is a second copy. |

### Not needed now

| Item | When it becomes relevant |
|---|---|
| Sentry, PostHog, GrowthBook accounts | Optional. Add one when Vercel's built-in tools stop being enough (D13). |
| Cookie-consent review per country | Only if non-essential cookies or pixels are added (D14). |
| Data protection impact assessment | Before the AI assistant launches (Phase 3). |
| Accountant review of the VAT setup | Recommended before launch, not legally required. |
| Data processing agreements with Vercel and Supabase | Account-level; if your team and organisation already have them, this project is covered. |
